## Snapshot
Family of Apps (Facebook, Instagram, WhatsApp, Threads, Messenger) reaches 3.5B+ daily users; ~98% of revenue is advertising. Reality Labs (Quest, AI glasses, Horizon) is the secondary, loss-making segment. The stock is now a hyperscaler/AI-capex name as much as a digital-ads name: META is building frontier models inside Meta Superintelligence Labs (MSL), spending on NVDA GPUs + AMD + Broadcom custom silicon (>1GW own ASIC rolling out per Q1'26 call), and signing multi-year cloud deals. Chain position: ad platform + AI hyperscaler / GPU + ASIC buyer + power/data-center demand driver.

## At a glance — product · buyer · supplier
| | |
|---|---|
| **Sells (top 3)** | 1) Family of Apps advertising (~98% of revenue; FB/IG/WhatsApp/Threads) · 2) Meta AI / GenAI ad & agent products (value-optimization >$20B run-rate) · 3) Reality Labs hardware (AI glasses, Quest) |
| **Main buyer(s)** | Advertisers — largest segment e-commerce/DTC (China-heavy, tariff-exposed); 8M+ advertisers on GenAI tools; plus consumers/businesses for hardware & Meta AI |
| **Key suppliers** | NVDA + AMD GPUs, Broadcom custom MTIA ASIC (multi-GW from 2027), memory/components (cost driving capex), power/DC (own datacenters + multi-year third-party cloud deals) |

## Current state (latest quarter — Q2 FY26, reported 2026-07-29)

We will deliver 3 GENERATIONS — we have LINE OF SIGHT OF 3GW THROUGH 2028.**"* And in the FQ4 bridge: *"**META custom MTIA for inference and recommendation** — at XPU and AI networking will **TRIPLE in Q4**"*, named as one of the three drivers of Broadcom's $21.7bn FQ4 AI guide alongside Ironwood to [[ANTHROPIC]] and v8i to [[GOOG]].
- ➤ 🔴 **SCALE CHECK, AND IT IS THE USEFUL PART: 3GW through 2028 puts Meta's custom-silicon programme at roughly **a third of [[ANTHROPIC]]'s 10GW 2028 line of sight** and **well under [[OPENAI]]'s >5GW in 2028 alone** on Broadcom's own numbers. Meta is a real MTIA customer but it is NOT in the same size class as the two labs — and Hock explicitly named Anthropic #1 and OpenAI #2 among his six XPU customers, which puts Meta third or lower.** ➤ **Read against Meta's own disclosed capex: this is the first time the wiki can put a GW number on the in-house-silicon share of that spend.**
- **The application is specified: *"custom MTIA for INFERENCE AND RECOMMENDATION"* — not training.** ⚠️ **Which means Meta's training fleet remains merchant-GPU, and the MTIA programme is not (yet) evidence of merchant displacement at the frontier.**

**🆕🔴 2026-09-02 (PRIMARY — THE CALL ITSELF, WHICH UPGRADES THE RELAY DIRECTLY ABOVE) — THE MTIA PROGRAMME GETS A HORIZON (*"BETWEEN NOW AND THE END OF 2027"*), A Q4 START DATE FOR PRODUCTION SHIPMENTS, AND — THE ITEM WORTH MOST TO THIS PAGE — BROADCOM PUBLICLY SORTS META INTO THE **FOUR OF SIX XPU CUSTOMERS THAT *"CAN FUND IT THEMSELVES"***, AGAINST THE TWO LABS THAT NEED AN SPV.** *(Broadcom mgmt (Hock Tan / Amie Thuener), Q3 FY26 earnings call, 2026-09-02 (Bloomberg LIVE transcript).)* ⚠️ *Supplier commentary about a customer — not a Meta disclosure, and no Meta capex, revenue or estimate on this page is changed by it.* ⚠️ *Source PDF had **NO TEXT LAYER** and was read via rendered page images; a Bloomberg **FINAL** transcript will supersede it.*
- **THE PROGRAMME, IN MANAGEMENT'S OWN WORDS, WITH THE TWO WORDS THE RELAY DROPPED: the partnership to deliver **MULTIPLE GENERATIONS of MTIA** *"remains **ON TRACK BETWEEN NOW AND THE END OF 2027**"*, with AVGO delivering **THREE generations of MTIA accelerators** and **line of sight to deploy 3GW through 2028**.** ➤ **The relay above carried the 3 generations and the 3GW; the primary adds the **CONTRACTED HORIZON — "between now and the end of 2027"** — which is the useful part, because it means the three generations are scheduled INSIDE a window Meta itself has refused to guide (no FY27 capex number exists). The 3GW runs to 2028; the DELIVERY commitment runs only to end-2027. Do not read the two dates as the same thing.**
- **AND THE Q4 START IS EXPLICIT: *"in Q4 FY26, **production shipments** of [[META]]'s **custom media accelerator (MTIA) optimized for INFERENCE AND RECOMMENDATION AT SCALE**"* begin — named by Hock as one of the three drivers of Broadcom's $21.7bn FQ4 AI guide, alongside Ironwood to [[ANTHROPIC]] and TPU v8 to [[GOOG]].** ➤ **So the ramp is a **CALENDAR-Q4-2026 EVENT, NOT A 2027 ONE** — it starts inside Broadcom's FQ4 (ending 1-Nov-2026), which lands just before Meta's own Q3 print and inside the FY26 capex guide of $130-145bn. ⚠️ **The application is again specified as INFERENCE AND RECOMMENDATION AT SCALE — not training** — which is the same limit the relay flagged and which the primary does nothing to soften.**
- 🔴 **THE NET-NEW ITEM FOR THIS PAGE, AND IT IS A BALANCE-SHEET COMPLIMENT: asked why only two of his six XPU customers get financing vehicles, Hock said the vehicles exist for [[OPENAI]] and [[ANTHROPIC]] because *"the **OTHER FOUR are FINANCIALLY SECURE, stable enough to FUND IT THEMSELVES**."* Meta is one of those four.** ➤ ✅ **A favourable contrast this page can use directly against its own capex/ROIC bear: Broadcom built a **>20GW XPV SPV platform with Apollo and Blackstone**, and drew a **$35bn first tranche**, to finance the labs' silicon — and told the market its hyperscaler customers, Meta among them, **need none of it**. Set against the financing thread already on this page (the Barclays issuance tracker, the ~95bp repricing between Meta's two deals, Susan Li's *"we don't have any plans to share"*), the read is that Meta's use of structured financing is a **cost-of-capital CHOICE**, not a funding necessity — which is precisely the distinction the equity-raise bear case elides.** ⚠️ **A supplier's characterisation of a customer's balance sheet, offered while defending its OWN credit exposure — self-interested in the direction of making its book look safe. It is not a credit opinion and nothing on this page's capex or financing lines changes on it.**
- **SCALE CHECK, RESTATED AGAINST THE CORRECTED LAB NUMBERS (the relay's version of these was built on figures the primary has since qualified): Broadcom's own ladder is **[[ANTHROPIC]] 1GW (2026) → 5GW (2027) → an *INCREMENTAL* 10GW line of sight (2028), all Anthropic-only** (*"the ten gigawatts is just for Anthropic"*, Q&A to Blayne Curtis, Jefferies) and **[[OPENAI]] 1.3GW in 2027 with line of sight to >5GW of Jalapeño and its successor**. Against those, **META's 3GW through 2028 is roughly a FIFTH of the Anthropic 2027-28 ladder** and it makes Meta, on Hock's explicit ranking (Anthropic #1 in FY27-28, OpenAI #2), **third or lower among six XPU customers**.** ➤ **Meta is a real custom-silicon buyer and not a top-tier one. ⚠️ But the comparison is only about BROADCOM'S BOOK — it says nothing about Meta's total accelerator spend, which remains overwhelmingly merchant GPU. Do not read "a fifth of Anthropic" as a statement about relative compute.**

- **Muse Voice Transcribe**, from Meta Superintelligence Labs, with the technical mechanism given: *"Following the settlement, the market is looking for META to **ship quick into year-end** and this is a step in the right direction. **Audio perception models ensure conversational fluidity**, which should help grow adoption of their AI tools including Meta AI… A standout capability is that the model has **'adaptive delay'**, enabled with reinforcement learning where **word error rate (WER) reward and a delay reward are combined MULTIPLICATIVELY**. With adaptive delay, Muse Voice Transcribe achieves **the Pareto front on the speed-accuracy trade-off** measured by time to final transcription"* (Bernstein TMT spec sales · **Tyler Seidman**, 2026-09-02). ➤ ⚠️ **Seidman's framing — *"a step in the right direction POST-SETTLEMENT"* — is the thing to track: the bar Meta is being held to is shipping cadence, not benchmark wins.**



**🆕🔴🔴 2026-08-27 — BofA HOSTS A DEDICATED POST-SETTLEMENT CALL WITH JUSTIN POST, AND IT IS THE MOST COMPLETE FRAMING OF THE AG SETTLEMENT ON THIS PAGE — INCLUDING THE ACCRUAL ARITHMETIC, WHAT IS *NOT* SETTLED, AND FOUR PUSHBACKS THE BUY-SIDE IS ACTUALLY MAKING.** (**BofA Securities · Justin Post**, client call hosted by Annie, 2026-08-27, the day after the settlement announcement. **PT $800.** ⚠️ Meta posted its own call transcript publicly; this is BofA's read of it plus their own view.) [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_08_27_meta_call_baml_27_ago_26.html)
- 🔴 **THE $10bn ACCRUAL IS NOT THE SETTLEMENT SIZE, AND THIS IS THE NUMBER MOST LIKELY TO BE MIS-QUOTED: *"there's a couple questions on why it's not 18 billion. It **is the NET PRESENT VALUE of the FIRST TRANCHE, which I think is 12 or 13. So the NPV on the 12.7 is 10 billion.** And **if the OTHER COMPANIES come to an agreement that UNLOCKS THE OTHER 5 BILLION, they would accrue that THEN.**"*** ➤ **So: a headline of roughly $18bn = a ~$12.7bn first tranche (accrued at $10bn NPV) + ~$5bn CONTINGENT on other platforms settling. Anyone modelling an $18bn cash charge is wrong on both size and timing, and the contingent leg is outside Meta's control.**
- 🔴 **WHY THE STOCK CARED, IN ONE WORD — CERTAINTY: *"I think the stock for some LONG-ONLYS and RETAIL investors was somewhat **UNINVESTABLE** when they were hearing numbers like **200 billion or even 1.4 trillion**. I don't think the buy side believed anything like that was going to happen, but **with the jury, I think people were very nervous about a NEGATIVE OCTOBER CATALYST.**"* Settled with **48 states — all AGs except FLORIDA and NEW MEXICO** — and it *"puts to bed the potential claims around CAMBRIDGE."*** ➤ **The removed event is specifically the October jury verdict, not the litigation as a whole.**
- ⚠️⚠️ **WHAT IS *NOT* SETTLED, AND THE PAGE SHOULD CARRY IT: the SCHOOL DISTRICT cases and INDIVIDUAL USER (personal-injury) cases remain — *"this is SERIOUS litigation. And as our expert said, **this could last YEARS AND YEARS**."* **BUT the burden is different and it favours Meta: *"the plaintiffs have the burden of showing CAUSATION, which is UNLIKE the AG cases. They have to show they suffered injury, and the injury was caused by Meta's product. Meta's products ALONE."*** plus *"very strong defenses under **SECTION 230 and the FIRST AMENDMENT**."* ➤ **Post expects possible consolidation into a class action, *"several years away"* from resolution.**
- **THE BUSINESS IMPACT IS ARGUED TO BE DE MINIMIS, WITH THE NUMBERS: **teens are ~1% of revenue** (reiterating Zuckerberg's trial testimony); the settlement imposes a **2-hour limit across both apps** against average teen Instagram usage of *"about an HOUR a day"*; and *"there's **NO BIG CHANGE in Meta's ALGOS**"* — personalisation survives. On the parental-override question: *"if teens are really 1% of their revenues and your heavy users are, I don't know, half of that, **you're talking about HALF A POINT**."*
- 🔴 **THE FOUR PUSHBACKS BofA SAYS IT HEARS CONSTANTLY — THIS IS THE MOST USEFUL PART OF THE CALL BECAUSE IT IS A MAP OF THE ACTUAL BEAR CASE:** **(1)** the October jury / December-January judge overhang — *"that's off the table"* now; **(2)** poor reception for the **personal AI product launch** (timing now *"late August… or September"*) — *"people don't know if it'll be DIFFERENTIATED versus [[OPENAI]] and what you could do with Gemini"*, and Meta AI *"had a nice little run in April and May but hasn't really gotten big user traction"*; **(3)** scepticism that even a leading frontier model moves the needle — *"first you got to build out the model and then maybe start having some LICENSING revenues show up, but I think people are UNCONVINCED"*; **(4)** 🔴 **CAPEX.**
- 🔴🔴 **THE CAPEX PUSHBACK, QUANTIFIED, AND IT IS THE NUMBER THIS PAGE MOST NEEDS: *"There were articles from **REUTERS** saying they could be adding **up to SEVEN GIGAWATTS of capacity next year**. **If you assume 40 billion a gigawatt, that could put you in the MID-2s** even up from **145 THIS YEAR**."* Post's own view: *"I think that's probably **UNLIKELY to be that high**"*, but on the survey base *"if the number's **200 billion, which is kind of the MINIMUM**… we'd think they would probably need to **RAISE SOME CAPITAL** to fund it, or they're really going to keep doing pretty large deals with **BLACKROCK and others**."*** ⚠️⚠️ **UNIT DISCIPLINE, PER THE STANDING `$/watt` RULE ON THIS WIKI: the "$40bn a gigawatt" here is a TOTAL BUILD-COST-per-GW assumption used to gross up a capex number — it is NOT [[NVDA]]-revenue-per-GW, even though NVDA's own Vera Rubin frame is coincidentally also ~$40bn/GW. The two must never be netted or chained. And the 7GW is REUTERS reporting, not company guidance.** ➤ **Working range from the call: FY26 capex ~$145bn → 2027 somewhere between ~$200bn (described as the buy-side MINIMUM) and the mid-$200bns (the Reuters-derived bull/bear extreme). Financing is the constraint, and the call flags *"a lot of requests for the amount of EXTERNAL FINANCING that's NOT NECESSARILY ON THE BALANCE SHEET, how to quantify that."***
- ⚠️ **ON WHETHER THEY RAISE EQUITY, THE ANSWER IS EXPLICITLY TIME-LIMITED: asked to confirm the at-earnings message that no near-term capital raise was planned, Post said *"I think that's fair for **THREE MONTHS**, but I wouldn't say that's HARD AND FAST over the next year."*** **And on the irreversibility of the commitment: *"all these commitments that they have you can read about in the K, both on **equipment purchases and/or leases**. **This is NOT something you can fix with a 500, 5,000-person layoff.** It buys you some time, but they seem to be ALL IN."***
- 🔴 **THE HOUSE VIEW, AND IT IS A SENTIMENT-ASYMMETRY CALL RATHER THAN AN EARNINGS CALL: *"Meta is now **15 MONTHS into this AI investment cycle**. We're on the cusp of multiple products, and the stock is facing **VERY NEGATIVE SENTIMENT**, which we think puts it in a relatively good position."* The valuation argument: *"we've published that **if we assume a 15 MULTIPLE on the core business, the CAPACITY INVESTMENT HAS CLOSE TO ZERO VALUE in the stock**, while other stocks are getting significant value for their capacity."*** ➤ **That is the cleanest statement of the Meta bull case available: you are not paying for the AI build. It is also a testable claim about the multiple, not about the products.**
- **AND THE DOWNSIDE IS BOUNDED BY TWO NAMED PLAN Bs: *"if the products don't work, yes, the stock could be in limbo for another six to nine months, but Meta would either **SCALE BACK CAPACITY GROWTH next year** or **START LEASING CAPACITY TO OTHERS**, which are pretty good plan Bs."*** ⚠️ **Both plan Bs are read-throughs elsewhere on this wiki — a Meta capacity scale-back is a direct [[NVDA]] demand risk, and Meta leasing capacity out makes it a neocloud competitor. Neither is in anyone's model.**
- **On the muted +1.5% stock reaction: *"I think it reflects the **UNCERTAINTY ABOUT THEIR AI ROIC**… if they really believed in their AI position and this was the only thing holding people back, you would have had a much bigger reaction."*

**🆕🔴🔴 2026-08-26 — THE OAKLAND TEEN-SAFETY CASE SETTLED BEFORE THE JURY GOT IT. ~$18BN OVER TEN YEARS, 52 STATE AGs, AND ONLY ~70% OF IT IS UNCONDITIONAL. THIS IS THE SINGLE BIGGEST OVERHANG ON THIS PAGE COMING OFF — BUT IT IS NOT A FULL CLEARING EVENT AND BOTH COVERING HOUSES SAY SO.** (BofA Securities · **Justin Post** w/ Nitin Bansal, *"Meta settlement avoids jury verdict, opens door for AI catalysts"*, 2026-08-26 01:05PM EDT — **BUY maintained, PO US$810 (established 30-Jul-2026), price US$570.05** · Barclays · **Ross Sandler** w/ Alex Hughes, Michael DiSanto, Alexander Kessinger, *"Quick Take on Today's Legal Settlement — First Look"*, released 26-Aug-26 20:19 GMT — **OVERWEIGHT, PT US$780, price (25-Aug-26) US$570.05, +36.8% upside, US Internet industry view POSITIVE**.)

**The terms, as the two houses describe them:**
- **BofA: an agreement with a bipartisan group of 52 US attorneys general covering teen-safety protections across Meta's applications, with a payment of ~$18bn (~$7/Meta share) in annual instalments over TEN YEARS.** The structure is the part that matters: **participating states receive ~70% (~$12.7bn); the remaining 30% (~$5.3bn) is released ONLY IF two conditions are met — (1) YouTube and TikTok implement a one-hour Daily Limit, Night Mode and age-assurance measures, and (2) YouTube and TikTok EACH pay an amount matching the 30% figure.**
- **Product remedies Meta agreed to: default daily usage limits, nighttime restrictions, muted notifications during school hours, expanded parental controls and enhanced age assurance.**
- **BofA: Meta expects to accrue a legal expense of ~$10BN IN 3Q'26.** ⚠️ **That is the P&L event and it lands in the quarter now in progress.**
- ⚠️⚠️ **THE TWO HOUSES DO NOT AGREE ON THE HEADLINE NUMBER AND THE PAGE IS NOT SMOOTHING IT: BofA says "~$18bn"; Barclays says "a maximum ~$17.1b." Barclays' figure is described as a maximum INCLUSIVE of the contingent portion, which is arithmetically hard to reconcile with BofA's $12.7bn + $5.3bn = $18bn. Barclays also notes the settlement "includes smaller fines/settlements for Cambridge Analytica and a few other issues," which may account for scope differences. UNRESOLVED — do not quote a single number off this page without the house attached.**

🔴 **THE IMPACT-ZONE SIZING IS THE MOST USEFUL NEW NUMBER, AND BARCLAYS DOES THE WORK: *"the impact zone for product changes is relatively small, as **~6% of IG DAUs and ~1% of FB DAUs are US teens**"* — sourced to discovery records — with 2021 Facebook Files figures of **~22mn Instagram and ~5mn Facebook US teenage DAUs** as the historical anchor. **Every US state and territory settled EXCEPT FLORIDA AND NEW MEXICO.** Census data implies **~37mn children aged 10-19 in the settling group, of which Barclays estimates "a bit over half are actually teenage users," putting the settlement at ~$900 PER TEENAGE DAU.** BofA independently corroborates the revenue-exposure point from a different source: *"revenue exposure is limited as **teens contribute 1% of rev** (per Mr. Zuckerberg's recent court testimony)."* ➜ **Two houses, two methods, same conclusion: the product remedies bite a single-digit share of users and ~1% of revenue. The settlement is a cash and headline event, not an engagement event — on the evidence available today.**

⚠️ **WHAT NEITHER HOUSE WILL CALL A CLEARING EVENT, AND THE LIST IS LONGER THAN THE SETTLEMENT:**
- **Barclays: *"the decision does not seem to resolve cases from private citizens or other government bodies… Age verification and other algorithmic design policies are still working through a number of different countries and courts, and it's not just a META issue… So while we would like to point to this as a clearing event, IT DOESN'T COMPLETELY CLEAR ALL AIRWAYS."***
- **BofA's four named risks: (1) *"settlement does not reduce liability for past actions"* — Meta *"continues to face THOUSANDS of personal-injury and school-district claims in multidistrict litigation, which could take several years to resolve and result in additional financial & other obligations"* (per BofA's own recent legal call); (2) the agreement *"may prompt regulators OUTSIDE THE US to pursue similar measures"*; (3) age-assurance and parental-control systems raise compliance and ongoing monitoring costs; (4) restrictive product features *"could reduce teen usage, impacting Meta's ADULT USER PIPELINE."*** ⚠️ **(4) is the only one of the four that is a long-duration fundamental risk rather than a cash risk, and it is the one the ~1%-of-revenue framing does NOT address: the teen cohort is the top of the funnel, not the monetised base.**

✅ **WHY BOTH HOUSES ARE NET-POSITIVE ANYWAY, AND IT IS THE SAME ARGUMENT: THE OVERHANG WAS BLOCKING THE AI RE-RATING.** **Barclays: *"the settlement shifts attention back to AI progress at MSL and away from the headline risk that has plagued META for a number of quarters."*** **BofA: *"the settlement reduces regulatory uncertainty, concerns on a large jury verdict, and will enable Meta stock to better reflect AI catalysts ahead."*** BofA then lists the six-month catalyst path it expects to do the re-rating: **(1) a personal AI product launch, (2) a top-tier LLM launch, (3) AI model licensing deals, (4) an AI capacity deal, (5) messaging ad monetization platforms** — adding that *"Street sentiment on Meta AI investments and ability to develop a compelling Personal AI product seems NEGATIVE, so a strong offering could reshape AI views."* ➜ **That list maps directly onto the 'Hatch'/'Watermelon' launch thread already on this page (08-25) — BofA's catalyst (1) IS Hatch. The two are the same bet stated by an insider leak and by a covering analyst in the same week.** ✅ **Financial flexibility explicitly preserved: BofA notes *"the $18bn settlement amount is LESS THAN FEARED and should have limited impact on Meta's financial flexibility to invest given the extended payment schedule"* — i.e. ~$1.8bn/yr against a $130-145bn FY26 capex guide.**

🔴 **THE COMPETITIVE ANGLE IS NOT A SIDE NOTE — IT IS DESIGNED IN.** **BofA: *"Settlement is structured so other platforms are LESS LIKELY TO HAVE COMPETITIVE ADVANTAGES"*** — the 30% contingent tranche only releases if YouTube and TikTok adopt the same daily limits, night mode and age assurance **and** pay matching amounts. ➜ **Meta has bought itself a mechanism that converts its own remedy into an industry standard, and it gets ~$5.3bn of its own payment back if the industry follows.** **BofA: *"[[GOOG]]'s YouTube will likely see pressure to implement similar teen safety measures and contribute to the State teen-safety fund"*; Snap was NOT among the platforms named in the contingent conditions but *"the agreement will likely increase pressure on Snap to adopt additional teen safety"* — and the tape agreed: **Snap −~9% on the day vs a flat S&P.*** ⚠️ **Barclays names the contingency counterparties slightly differently — "fellow Core Industry Members (TikTok, YouTube, Snap)" — i.e. it reads Snap as inside the contingency where BofA reads it as outside. Another unreconciled detail between the two notes; the primary settlement document was not read here.** ➜ **Cross-file to [[GOOG]].**

**🆕🔴 2026-08-25 — 'HATCH' AND 'WATERMELON' GET DATES, A PRICE POINT AND A LIST OF THIRD-PARTY SERVICES IT WILL DRIVE.** *"META reportedly plans to launch codename **'Hatch'**, a consumer version of the OpenClaw AI agent, in **late August or early September**, and its latest AI model **'Watermelon' in OCTOBER**"* (The Information, via MS TMT · Thomas Wigg and Barclays desk · Jeffrey Rand, both 2026-08-25).
- **Pricing:** *"Meta has considered **tiered subscriptions, with a premium tier as high as $199.99/month** and higher usage limits."*
- 🔴 **Scope — this is the part that matters for the read-through:** *"Hatch is being **trained to operate across third-party services including [[SHOP]]-adjacent commerce, DASH, ETSY, [[RDDT]], YELP and [[MSFT]] Outlook**, suggesting a broad **'do things for me' agent** rather than simply another Meta AI chatbot."* **[[RDDT]] traded +6% on 08-25 partly on being named in that list** (TMTB EOD Wrap, 2026-08-25).
- **Distribution:** *"**WhatsApp is becoming another agent distribution layer** — META is separately preparing a platform allowing users to integrate and chat with **third-party AI agents inside WhatsApp**, with a **limited rollout potentially beginning this week**."*
- **MS's framing is blunt: "META needs these AI product shots on goal to justify the spending, or a Neocloud deal."**

**🆕🔴 2026-08-25 — ISI PUTS THE FIRST SIZED NUMBER ON META SELLING COMPUTE EXTERNALLY, AND THEN ARGUES AGAINST IT.** *"ISI had a note out today saying they think **Meta could generate ~$11B of annualized revenue for every 0.5GW of AI capacity sold externally, adding roughly $1.44-2.16 of EPS, or ~4-6% versus 2027 consensus, with a potential ~$11-22B annual opportunity if Meta leans in**. But **Mahaney doesn't view this as a new neocloud-style business, arguing today's premium compute pricing should FADE as capacity expands and management remains focused on monetizing higher-margin 'INTELLIGENCE' rather than selling raw compute**"* (Evercore ISI · Mark Mahaney, relayed in TMTB EOD Wrap, 2026-08-25). ⚠️⚠️ **BASIS WARNING: "$11bn per 0.5GW" is REVENUE per GW (≈$22bn/GW) — it must NOT be netted against, or chained with, the $/GW BUILD-COST anchors in `_meta/assumptions.md`. Different denominators.** ⚠️ *Desk relay; the ISI note itself was not read here.*

**🆕 2026-08-25 — VALUATION AND THE SECOND OPTIONALITY LEG.** *"About the 5th or 6th time META has bounced off **$550**, and even the bears I talk to have to concede r/r is pretty skewed to upside there — you're getting **META at 16x street numbers and <15x buyside's $35-36**, before factoring in any contribution from selling compute. The other piece of optionality is bulls think META has a **$15B+ durable incremental revenue opportunity from BUSINESS AGENTS AND SUBSCRIPTIONS alone, with WhatsApp paid penetration still only ~2.5%**; Cloud/API monetization would be additional upside"* (TMTB EOD Wrap, 2026-08-25). **META +2% on 08-25, outperforming the other hyperscalers. Next catalyst: Meta Connect, Sept 23-24.** **Bernstein published a "META 2H setup (= +)" piece the same day** (Bernstein TMT · Tyler Seidman, 2026-08-25 — ⚠️ *headline only, body not read*). **Positioning context: META remains among the largest active overweight positions globally** (BofA strategy · Nigel Tupper, 2026-08-25).

**🆕 2026-08-18 (/run-inbox) — THE MTIA ROADMAP GETS ITS FIRST HARDWARE SPECS ON THIS PAGE: 300 → 500 IS A ~2× MEMORY AND >2× POWER STEP** (read-through from Wells Fargo · Aaron Rakers, "[[AVGO]] F3Q26 Preview", 2026-08-18 — an AVGO note; Meta appears as a custom-silicon customer, no META rating/PT).

**🆕🔴 2026-08-24 — META'S CONSUMER AGENT PLATFORM GETS A NAME, A LAUNCH WINDOW AND A PRICE POINT: "HATCH" IN WEEKS, MODEL "WATERMELON" IN OCTOBER, PREMIUM TIER CONSIDERED AT UP TO $199.99/MONTH.** (The Information · **Jyoti Mann**, "Meta Plans to Launch 'Hatch' AI Agent Platform in Coming Weeks", 2026-08-24 — *sourced to internal documents reviewed by The Information*)
- **The takeaway verbatim:** *"Meta Platforms plans to launch its **consumer version of the OpenClaw AI agent, dubbed HATCH internally, as soon as the next several weeks**, and is **targeting OCTOBER for its latest AI model, called WATERMELON**, according to internal documents reviewed by The Information."*
- **The monetisation intent is explicit:** *"Hatch is part of… Mark Zuckerberg's ambitions to **monetize the company's enormous AI investments and diversify its revenue streams BEYOND ADVERTISING**. Meta has considered a **tiered pricing system**… including charging **up to $199.99 for a premium monthly subscription** for the product that would include **higher usage limits**."*
- 🔴 **Why this matters to the capex debate on this page rather than just to the product story: a usage-limited subscription tier is the first META product structure that converts inference spend into a DIRECT, metered consumer revenue line. Every prior justification for META's AI capex on this page has run through advertising efficiency — an indirect, hard-to-attribute channel. A $199.99/month tier is attributable by construction, and it is also the first place a token-cost line would show up in META's own gross margin.**
- ⚠️ **Evidence class: internal documents seen by a trade publication. No Meta confirmation, no announced launch date, and "considered" pricing is not announced pricing. Nothing here is guidance.**
- **Context this sits in:** META was among the **outperformers with the other hyperscalers (AMZN, GOOGL, MSFT)** on a **SOX −3%** day (Vital Knowledge · Adam Crisafulli, 2026-08-24), and **META Connect is confirmed for Sep 23-24** (JPM Market Intelligence AI/Tech catalysts, 2026-08-24) — 🔴 **which is the natural venue for a Hatch launch and sits inside the "coming weeks" window.**
- **On the silicon side, unchanged and worth restating against the above:** UBS's 08-24 AM work (on [[AVGO]]) still has **META custom compute FLAT through F2026**. ➜ **Meta is scaling a consumer inference product while its custom-silicon spend is modelled flat — so the near-term compute for Hatch has to come from merchant silicon, not from an in-house ramp.**
- **MTIA 300, as specified: 6× HBM3 stacks per accelerator (8.0 Gbps, 24/36GB per stack, 1 TB/s bandwidth per stack), in a scale-up domain of 16 accelerators.** Off Meta's own published MTIA roadmap, Wells flags the generational step: **HBM capacity rising from 216GB to 384–512GB, and TDP from 800W to 1,700W, between MTIA 300 and MTIA 500.** ➤ **Two consequences worth carrying. (1) A 16-accelerator scale-up domain is SMALL against NVL72-class racks — Meta's part is built for a different unit of work, which is consistent with an inference/ranking workload rather than frontier training, and it caps how far MTIA can substitute for merchant GPUs in Meta's own capex. (2) The 800W → 1,700W step is a power-and-cooling commitment on the same footprint, and it lands squarely on the [themes/800v-dc-power](themes/800v-dc-power.md) and liquid-cooling read-throughs.**
- **The commercial scale [[AVGO]] models against it: Meta MTIA revenue to Broadcom of $1.15bn FY26 → $12.0bn FY27 → $25.8bn FY28 → $30.6bn FY29, on 0.10 / 1.00 / 2.15 / 2.50 GW deployed at ~$11.5–12.3bn per GW; program described as MTIA 2nm with an initial commitment to exceed 1GW and a multi-GW rollout scheduled through 2029, with Broadcom content spanning custom XPU, Ethernet switching, optical, PCIe and SerDes.** ⚠️ **These are AVGO's revenue estimates, i.e. Meta's SPEND on Broadcom silicon — not Meta's total accelerator capex and not a Meta disclosure. Do not read the GW ladder as Meta's fleet.** [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/81b52470-2170-4d31-a7b2-2d8f34f632ce.html)

**NEW / BULL 2026-08-18 — ADS ARE THE VERIFICATION ENGINE THAT CAN PAY FOR FRONTIER AI.** Ben Thompson argues that Meta can validate generated creative and matching at global scale through clicks and purchases; a few percentage points of relevance improvement are worth billions, independently justifying frontier-model spend. The counterweights are specific: Meta currently pays zero for most user-generated content, so company-generated AI content worsens that margin profile; management has not told the advertising ROI story well; and the >$100bn cumulative Oculus spend weakens the market's willingness to fund another open-ended project. Net: being off-frontier may be more reckless for a digital company, but the returns should be sold as ad economics, not as a vague superintelligence option (Ben Thompson, *Invest Like the Best*, 2026-08-18).

**🆕 2026-08-14 — NOWAK PUTS A FLOOR AND A CEILING ON META'S EARNINGS AND THE GAP BETWEEN THEM IS THE WHOLE STOCK: "LOW $30s" GAAP EPS AS A FLOOR OVER THE NEXT TWO YEARS, "$40+ IF THE PRODUCTS WORK."** (Morgan Stanley · **Brian Nowak**, GenAI-ROIC pod on AMZN / GOOGL / META, via Capstone desk summary + MS Tech Talk, 2026-08-14. ⚠️ **Podcast relay of research already folded on this page from the 08-12 note — treat as a restatement, NOT as independent confirmation. No rating, PT or published estimate change captured. The desk summary tags the figures "Analyst estimate with clear upside/downside."**)
- **The two numbers as relayed:** *"**Meta GAAP EPS (next 2 years): 'Low $30s' as a FLOOR. Potential for $40+ IF PRODUCTS WORK.**"* ➜ **A ~30% spread between floor and upside on a two-year view, with the swing explicitly attributed to PRODUCT MONETISATION rather than to capex, models or ad pricing. That is the cleanest statement on this page of where META's optionality actually sits — and it is falsifiable in a way the "manifesto" narrative below is not.**
- **The open-source strategy, framed as deliberate tiering rather than as ideology:** *"Meta continues to champion open weights (e.g. **new 30B model, Muse Spark 1.2 weights coming**), but is **likely to open-source 'SECOND BEST' models while keeping its FRONTIER models proprietary to preserve its moat**. **Future capex may be influenced by willingness to leverage HYPERSCALERS FOR DISTRIBUTION.**"* ➜ **Two live implications. First, "open the n-1, keep the frontier" is a testable prediction about what META releases next. Second, and more material for the capex line this page tracks: if META distributes through third-party hyperscalers rather than serving everything itself, its own build requirement falls — Nowak lists "the extent to which Meta will leverage hyperscalers for distribution versus building out its own infrastructure" as an explicit open debate.**
- **The bear leg, stated plainly:** *"**Despite significant investment and open-source efforts, if Meta's AI-driven products FAIL TO GAIN SUBSTANTIAL TRACTION AND MONETIZE, earnings growth could disappoint.**"* **What to monitor, per Nowak: "Announcements regarding Muse Spark DISTRIBUTION AGREEMENTS WITH HYPERSCALERS, performance of new open-source models, and details on 'watermelon' (next frontier model)."** ⚠️ **"Watermelon" is an internal codename as rendered in the desk summary; unverified, and the summary elsewhere garbles DeepSeek as "Deep Sea," so treat codenames from this source with care.** Separately in the week's tape, and consistent: **"Meta Fires Back At China's 15-Week AI Token Dominance With MUSE GLIMMER, which can fit inside a single GPU"** (headline in Susquehanna's weekly link digest, 2026-08-15 — ⚠️ **aggregator headline, not read; note "Muse Glimmer" and "Muse Spark" are different product names appearing in the same week and this page does not resolve which is which**).
- **A power datapoint on the META build, from the energy desk rather than the tech desk:** in a Jefferies behind-the-meter debate, the bull side notes **"ETR looking to increase META mega site in LA to up to 13 GW"** (Jefferies Energy/Power Sales · James Snyder, 2026-08-14). ⚠️ **Utility-side chatter relayed by a sales desk, unsourced, and a 13GW single site would be extraordinary — logged as a flag to verify against Entergy filings, NOT adopted. See [themes/ai-datacenter-power.md](themes/ai-datacenter-power.md) for the regulatory backdrop that makes the behind-the-meter route relevant.**

**🆕🔴 2026-08-11 — ZUCKERBERG TOLD A REPORTER *WHY* HE PUBLISHED THE 6,500-WORD AI MANIFESTO NOW, AND THE ANSWER IS THE MOST BULLISH MODEL SIGNAL META HAS GIVEN SINCE THE LLAMA RESET: he believes Meta is "VERY CLOSE to having substantially stronger models" and wanted his values on record before they arrive. META +1%.**
- **The scoop:** *"Zuckerberg told me the immediate reason he decided to **publish his philosophy now** is that Meta believes it is **'very close to having substantially stronger models,'** and he wants people to understand his values before those models arrive"* (Alex Heath / Sources, via TMTB EOD Wrap and MS TMT desk, both 2026-08-11). He apparently **wrote the longer 6,500-word version before the WSJ op-ed two weeks ago**, adding a defence of **model distillation** late in the process (Jefferies / Rankie Wong, 2026-08-11).
- **🔴 Why it is a bigger deal than a founder talking up his own company — there are now shipping artefacts behind it.** *"The prevailing view of Meta's AI effort is that it is still chasing [[ANTHROPIC]] and [[OPENAI]] after its Llama reset, the creation of Meta Superintelligence Labs, and a hiring spree that cost billions. **There are starting to be signs of what he predicts is coming on the model front**, though. Meta's last **Muse Spark** release was well received, and **Muse Glimmer is a new 30-BILLION-PARAMETER model designed to run on a SINGLE CONSUMER GPU**; Meta's own tests show it **outperforming similarly sized Gemma and Qwen models on several agentic benchmarks**. **Muse Spark 1.2**, whose weights Meta says it will release in the coming weeks, is a **coding model** that Meta itself describes as a step **"toward the frontier,"** with larger models still to come"* (TMTB, 2026-08-11).
- **The unreleased model is the real test, and two independent commentators say so: MS's desk calls the unnamed frontier model "Watermelon" and treats releases as "a significant near-term catalyst for perceptions of META's competitive positioning"; Spyglass's M.G. Siegler views "Glimmer and Spark as very good, but the unreleased 'Watermelon' frontier model as THE REAL TEST (also, unlikely to be open-sourced if it's genuinely competitive)"** (MS TMT desk; Spyglass via JPM / Joshua Meyers, both 2026-08-11). ➜ **That last parenthesis is the sharpest point of the day: if Watermelon is genuinely frontier-class, the open-weights strategy Zuckerberg just wrote 6,500 words defending may not survive contact with it.**
- **The manifesto's concrete commitments, per WSJ (via JPM, 2026-08-11):** framing Meta's AI strategy around **open-weight models and community investment**, including **a new $1 BILLION FUND FOR DATA-CENTER HOST COMMUNITIES**, the open-weight release of **Muse Spark 1.2**, and **Muse Glimmer**. ⚠️ **JPM's read is unsentimental and worth keeping: the essay "comes as Meta lags behind frontier labs in spending, and against a backdrop of investor frustration (Meta's stock sold off in July over vague cloud-business plans) and rising local opposition to data-center buildout. The essay reads in part as a POLICY AND PR PLAY — positioning openness as the 'safer' alternative to closed rivals — but LACKS A CLEAR NEAR-TERM MONETIZATION STORY to match spending."** Spyglass is harsher, calling the approach *"opportunistic, capitalizing on the political backlash against Chinese 'open' models."*
- **DIGITIMES frames the strategic bet plainly: "Meta bets open, on-device model can win AI power struggle… trying to turn open-source AI into a strategic weapon rather than a side project"** (2026-08-11). ➜ **Note the specific vector: Glimmer at 30B on a single consumer GPU is an on-device play, which is a different competitive axis from frontier scale — and the one place Meta's distribution is an actual advantage.**
- **Also logged: the [[CRWV]] concentration thread got no worse but no better — Citi's earlier point that ~1/3 of CoreWeave's backlog is tied to Meta and that "Meta Compute" makes new Meta-CRWV deals unlikely stands unchanged, while CRWV's Q2 (2026-08-11) disclosed a deliberately enterprise-weighted new-customer list. Separately, JPM TECH SKETCH flagged "META Watermelon Soon?" and a RIOT cloud deal with a possible Meta angle** (JPM / Mark Schilsky, 2026-08-11) — ⚠️ **the RIOT deal was subsequently attributed to [[ANTHROPIC]] ($9.1bn, 191MW, Rockdale TX), so the Meta speculation there did not hold.**

**🆕 2026-08-09/10 — a $567m child-safety judgment, and a named appearance in the guardrail-breach story.**
- **A New Mexico judge ordered Meta Platforms to pay $567 million in a child safety case** (The Information, 2026-08-10). Cash-immaterial at Meta's scale; the read is regulatory/precedent, into a US political calendar where platform child-safety liability is already live.
- **Meta is named alongside OpenAI and Anthropic** in the reporting that **models had jumped their guardrails to breach other companies' systems** — the revelation that triggered OpenAI's pause of Astra on cyber-capability grounds, days before the **White House AI-framework review with AI companies on Tuesday** (The Information, 2026-08-10). Cross-ref [OPENAI.md](OPENAI.md), [ANTHROPIC.md](ANTHROPIC.md).

- **★ THE SELL-SIDE REACTION — stock −8 to −9%, and it was the RHETORIC, not the numbers (2026-07-30).** The print was mixed-to-in-line and the guides were better-than-feared (Q3 revenue $61-64B "**smack in line with Buyside expectations**"; the FY26 opex ceiling $169B and the FY26 capex ceiling $145B **both untouched**) — and the stock still fell hard. JPM's desk titled it "**META Q2 'Trust Me, Bro'**": investors were "unsurprised that the stock was down, but were largely **surprised at the magnitude**"; the most-cited complaint was that Zuckerberg "wasn't specific enough in his outlook for a range of new AI products," with adjectives "**underwhelming**" and "**generic**" — "his prepared remarks amounted to '**trust me**' – and in this market that isn't nearly enough." **2027 EPS estimates barely moved (most remain $33-36, maybe down a low-single-digit %)** — i.e. this is a multiple/narrative event, not an estimate event. Susan Li's comment about **growing compute capacity in 2028** "seemed to spook some people… viewed as a negative given that we still don't know how exactly big the 2027 Capex number will ultimately be." Schilsky's own read stays constructive-with-patience: investors are "layering in all of the Opex and Capex related to Zuckerberg's AI largesse **without modeling in related Revenue**… it won't matter until he executes. It's coming. Be patient" — and he expects a [[MSFT]]-style re-rating moment "in the coming months," while noting the **MSL-vs-FRL déjà vu** and rejecting the analogy ("AI in all forms demonstrably represents a massive TAM. You could never have said that about the metaverse"). (JPM · Mark Schilsky desk, 2026-07-30)
  - **JPM research (Doug Anmuth) — Neutral, NEW Dec-2027 PT $640** on ~18x 2028E GAAP EPS of **$35.44**; **2027 capex estimate now $243B (+70% y/y)** with top-line estimates largely unchanged, "thereby putting **greater pressure on FCF**." On the substance: "we did **not come away learning much incremental on the developer API, consumer/business agents, or potential plans to directly monetize compute**," and no 2027 capex commentary beyond maximising 2026-27 capacity with more flexibility in 2028+. (JPM · Doug Anmuth, 2026-07-30)
  - **GS GIR:** positives were ad revenue +28% on broad-based engagement/monetisation plus AI ad tools — **Advantage+ at $75B ARR in Q2** — and the AI-monetisation framework (enterprises, model commercialisation, longer-term compute monetisation) plus subscriptions (Meta One). Negatives: capital intensity "remains elevated"; **Q2 GAAP expenses above GSe/Street with operating income BELOW**, on higher employee comp, infrastructure, legal and **3P token costs** (a new cost line to track); and live legal/regulatory risk — the **$2.4B litigation charge** plus disclosure of active matters that "could result in a material negative impact." (GS GIR via GS TMT Today, 2026-07-30)
  - **Downgrade: META → Neutral (President Capital Management, 2026-07-30).** Asia read-across was split: UBS's APAC desk found "Microsoft and Meta **monetization diverged** on their implications for supply chain sentiment" (UBS · Randy Abrams, 2026-07-30), while JPM's APAC thematics concluded "**META & MSFT: results & guidance not enough to change the AI narrative**" (JPM · Matthew See, 2026-07-30).
- **⚠ THE FY27 CAPEX NUMBER IS NOW A BASIS ARGUMENT, NOT A FORECAST ARGUMENT — and the house mark ($142B) sits at the extreme low end of it (Rothschild & Co Redburn · Rowan Adley, 2026-07-30).** FY26 capex was nudged to $130-145B from $125-145B — "a **floor raise** reflecting H1 actuals, better visibility and likely some BOM inflation" — and the more telling fact is that "**the FY26 ceiling did NOT go up despite component inflation**." The gross-build arithmetic: **1H26 spend $50.9B leaves ~$87B for 2H at the guide midpoint → annualise to $175B before ANY 2027 growth**; with Susan Li direct on maximising 2026/27 and still demand-constrained, "it could point to **$180-200bn or 30-45% YoY growth**." **BUT** the **BlackRock JV moves spend off Meta's balance sheet, "so reported could end up (potentially a lot) lower."** That is the crux for anyone marking a number: Redburn's own Dom Ball carries **$142B for FY27**, and Adley flags that against a 2026 midpoint of $137.5B this "implies **JVs absorb essentially ALL of the growth in gross build**, which would be an aggressive assumption off one announced deal (and the guide basis already includes finance lease principal, so **JVs are the only lever getting you there**)." Range on the page is therefore **$142B (Redburn, reported) → $180-200B (Redburn, gross) → $243B (JPM Anmuth)** — reconcile by basis before comparing. **Generalisable read-through, now folded into [hyperscaler-capex](themes/hyperscaler-capex.md): both META (off-BS JVs) and [[MSFT]] (lease reclassification) now have mechanisms that flatter reported capex optics, so "reported capex is becoming a WORSE proxy for silicon demand" — track cash PP&E / gross build instead.**
- **Compute monetization — META would rather sell INTELLIGENCE than raw compute, which keeps the merchant-compute market tighter (Rothschild/Adley, 2026-07-30).** Alongside "demand constrained for the foreseeable" and "receiving **premium bids** for its compute," management's stated preference is to sell intelligence over raw capacity — "lots of conclusions to draw, but one positive for semis is it **helps keep the compute market tighter for longer if META decides not to play**." Read-through to [[CRWV]] / [[NBIS]]: less near-term competitive supply than the 07-01 "Meta Compute" headlines implied (see Changelog 2026-07-01 for the original framing).
- **Financing decision explicitly deferred to the 2027 planning cycle.** Susan Li, asked directly about an equity raise: "we don't have anything… we don't have any plans to share… as we plan for our investments in 2027, I think we will **certainly evaluate all of the financing options**. But we will have more to share as we go into '27. For now, again, **our operating cash flow is really the primary pillar** by which we are financing all of our ambitions. We also again have… been **raising investment-grade debt**. We've **looked at these partnerships**. I think we will continually be evaluating what is the right funding source and what is the right cost of capital." (via JPM · Mark Schilsky quoting the Q2'26 earnings follow-up, 2026-07-30)
- **Q2 FY26 print — first take (8-K Ex-99.1 press release, 2026-07-29; call transcript **NOW INGESTED 2026-07-30** — see the call bullets immediately below; sell-side reaction and stock move **NOW INGESTED 2026-07-30** — see the reaction bullet below and the fresh `## Intra-quarter` window):** Revenue **$60.80B +28% y/y** (+27% cc) — within the $58-61B guide, +2.2% above midpoint, above Street $60.2B and BofA's $60.6B model, a hair under the $61B UBS buyside bogey. Ad revenue $59.36B +27% — **impressions +14%, price/ad +12%: growth mix rotated from volume to price** (impressions decelerated in every region from +19% WW in Q1; US&C pricing accelerated to +20%, a five-quarter high — see the regional decomposition table below). GAAP EPS **$6.18 (−13%)** vs the $7.53 buyside GAAP bogey — but total costs of $42.03B (+55%) carry **$2.40B of legal-proceedings charges + $1.18B of May-RIF severance** ($3.58B pretax one-offs; ex-items EPS ≈ $7.35-7.50 DERIVED, depending on charge deductibility); OI $18.8B, **31% margin** (43% LY); tax 16%. **Q3'26 guide $61-64B = +19-25% y/y** (~1% FX headwind). **FY26 capex NARROWED to $130-145B** (from $125-145B — a low-end raise, not the $135-150B full raise BofA floated); FY26 total expenses $165-169B (low end raised on the legal charge); tax 15-17% remaining quarters; OI still guided above FY25. Cash items: Q2 capex $31.08B, **FCF $784M**, OCF $31.9B, cash $90.3B vs LT debt $83.7B, dividends $1.35B; DAP 3.60B +3% (June). (META 8-K Ex-99.1, 2026-07-29)
- **Q2 FY26 CALL — the ingest that closes the 07-29 PENDING markers. Headline: management gave the enterprise/compute leg a far fuller shape but REFUSED the one number the print was set up to deliver — 2027 capex (META Q2'26 earnings call, 2026-07-29):** Zuckerberg framed the opportunity in three parts — (i) "our investments in AI are accelerating every major part of our core business," (ii) "new personal agents that will be the foundation for our next wave of products and revenue lines," (iii) **"a large enterprise opportunity to sell to businesses, including APIs, Business Agents, potentially selling compute directly, and other services that we're building for large customers."** On the capex question Nowak asked directly, Susan Li: **"we aren't providing a specific outlook for 2027 CapEx at this time. Infrastructure planning remains highly dynamic. And even this year, there are a range of outcomes embedded in our outlook."** So the three-way FY27 capex spread this page carried into the print — Redburn ~$145bn (07-26/27) / Street–JPM ~$200-205bn / Bernstein's "**I'm hearing +$250B is now the base case**" whisper (07-27) — is left **UNRESOLVED**, and the buyside bogey of $200-225bn (JPM desk, 07-28) gets no management anchor. What she did give is the planning posture: **"our current plans are geared towards maximizing 2026 and 2027 capacity,"** with 2028+ handled by "laying down data center and network foundations to accommodate future server decisions" so the chip/big-ticket commitments are deliberately deferred — "we're really focusing on flexibility… land and power, but to really make the actual decisions about buying chips and other big-ticket items further in the future" — plus the standing preference **"we believe near-term capacity is more valuable than long-term capacity."** **No equity raise was announced** (the page's standing near-term clearing catalyst): the funding answer was debt + partnerships — Li, **"we've also been evolving our capital structure in recent years to include a greater mix of debt as we work to bring down our cost of capital… we have generally found it prudent to continue adding cost-efficient, long-duration sources of capital,"** citing the **BlackRock strategic venture for a new 1GW data center in El Paso, Texas (announced 07-28, explicitly framed on the call as "part of our Meta Compute effort")** as "an example of the partnerships that we can structure to complement our approach to building infrastructure capacity." All guides were restated exactly as the 8-K carried them (Q3 $61-64B on ~1% FX headwind; FY26 expenses $165-169B with the low end raised for the $2.4B legal charge; capex **narrowed** to $130-145B; tax 15-17%; OI above 2025) — **no hard number on this page is superseded by the call.**
- **The compute-demand language went the OPPOSITE way to the "Meta Compute = overcapacity" bear — management is on the record as DEMAND-CONSTRAINED (META Q2'26 earnings call, 2026-07-29):** asked point-blank by Ken Gawrelski (WF) whether "maximize '26 and '27 capacity" was a demand or a supply statement, Li: **"we are today and expect to be in the sort of foreseeable future demand-constrained. That really includes our core business too, where there are — we still have numerous ROI-positive places that we would put Compute toward if we had it."** Supporting lines from the prepared remarks: **"The industry has underbuilt historically for the wave of AI adoption, making existing capacity, including our own, extremely valuable"**; **"we believe that overall industry capacity is going to remain tight for the foreseeable future"**; and Zuckerberg on the sell-while-buying paradox Anmuth pressed — **"the high-level observation is that there's just nowhere near enough Compute for all the demand."** This is first-party management corroboration of MS/Joe Moore's "excess demand, not excess supply" read (2026-07-06) and of SemiAnalysis's 07-02 "procurement will ACCELERATE" thesis, and it extends this page's compute-demand ladder one more rung: build-ahead (2024) → "compute-starved" (Q3'25) → "we continue to underestimate our compute needs" (Q1'26) → **"demand-constrained… numerous ROI-positive places that we would put Compute toward if we had it" (Q2'26)**. The tension is left ON the page, unresolved: the same call that says there is nowhere near enough compute also markets compute externally.
- **Meta Compute from management's own mouth — "offers at a significant premium over what we paid," but selling INTELLIGENCE beats selling compute (META Q2'26 earnings call, 2026-07-29):** Zuckerberg said twice that Meta is **"getting a lot of offers for Compute at a significant premium over what we paid for it"** / "quite a number of offers at a meaningful premium over what we paid for the Compute." The margin hierarchy he set is the important nuance for every $/GW sizing on this page (BofA $10-40bn/GW with a $5bn'27 / $11bn'28 placeholder; MS $40/Watt ≈ $40bn/GW → $3+/8% of '28 EPS; Citi ~$50bn/GW; WF ~$20bn per GW leased): **"we believe that there will continue to be a significantly higher margin on selling intelligence rather than selling Compute directly… I also think it would be foolish to basically just sell all of the Compute and take a short-term profit"** when you can build intelligence on top, "which will be a kind of a multiple and that compounds the value of the Compute." Asked by Nowak which leg scales first to "showcase quantifiable material ROIC for investors" in '26/'27, he **declined to rank any of them**: "I'm actually quite optimistic that we're going to see meaningful growth in all of these areas. And I think we'll have more to share soon on a number of them." Li's framing: enterprise offerings "have the potential to take multiple forms… agentic tools, our API or monetizing compute directly, given outsized market demand… remaining nimble about these opportunities will help us fund our build-out more efficiently, while preserving our strategic flexibility to have the compute when we need it." **⚠️ What was NOT said: no Anthropic deal, no SpaceX-type contract, no external compute-sale agreement and no dollar of compute revenue was announced or quantified** — so SemiAnalysis's 07-02/07-15 "expect a ~$10B Anthropic SpaceX-type deal, watch for the 90-day clawback" tell and the JPM/BofA (07-20) "reportedly leasing compute to Anthropic" reports remain **UNCONFIRMED by management**, and the page's Meta-Compute revenue placeholders stay as broker estimates only. On the go-to-market gap Sheridan pressed, Zuckerberg conceded the split: Business Agents are "quite a natural opportunity" as an extension of the existing advertiser/SMB motion ("just like the ad system, effectively, we will get paid when we deliver results for those businesses"), but coding/productivity/enterprise services are **"somewhat of a new muscle than we have historically had"** — a direct echo of the Archera ex-AWS operator's 07-13 "no enterprise sales motion" caveat. Custom silicon was framed as a capital-return lever, not just supply: "strategic investments in areas like our internal custom silicon effort, which will provide long-term strategic flexibility and supply chain leverage. This will be helpful in driving better returns on those long-term investments."
- **Ads AI — the quantified in-the-stack gains the "33% growth is pre-AI-product" bear was waiting for (META Q2'26 earnings call, 2026-07-29):** Li introduced the **"Meta generative recommender," explicitly called "a paradigm shift in how our ad system works" — "Rather than scoring every possible ad individually, we are now using LLMs to reason about ad content and user preferences together, and predict the best ad for each person"** — with the first generative model already deployed **into the ads retrieval system** ("notable improvements in ads performance"). The numbers: early LLM user-preference pilots drove **+1% app-event conversions on Instagram**, and the new user-understanding models combined with **GEM ads-ranking plus sequence learning generated +8.3% ad clicks and a +15.7% uplift in conversions on Facebook**. **Advantage+ AI-powered end-to-end solutions reached "over $75 billion in annual revenue run rate this quarter"** — the arc on this page now reads value-optimization >$20B (Q4'25) → end-to-end >$60B (Q3'25) → **>$75B (Q2'26)** — with **9 million small businesses using at least one AI creative tool** (from "8M+" at Q1'26) and image-generation adoption **more than doubling q/q**. Advertiser case studies given: **Movida** (Brazilian car rental, ~400 locations) deployed a WhatsApp Business Agent across the whole booking flow → **+44% daily bookings vs the prior-year period, with 85% of conversations resolved entirely by the AI agent without human assistance**; **Underneat** (India apparel) on Advantage+ sales campaigns + audience/placement/budget optimisation → **+13% incremental lift in purchases, +16% increase in ad-to-cart conversions**. Read for the page's central "is the growth pre-AI-product?" debate (BofA equity call, 06-11) and Linzer's pricing hesitation (UBS, 06-22): LLMs are now demonstrably **inside** the ads ranking/retrieval stack, and this is the first quarter management put those gains in double-digit-percent conversion terms.
- **Meta AI, Muse and the agent roadmap — the product cells the 8-K ingest left blank (META Q2'26 earnings call, 2026-07-29):** **"Since we rebuilt Meta AI and integrated Muse Spark, we have seen a 60% increase in the number of people interacting with the assistant each day, and that continues to grow quickly week-over-week"** (Zuckerberg) — a hard step up from the "double-digit % lift in sessions/user" the Q1'26 call gave. **Muse Spark 1.1 and Muse Image both shipped "in the last month"**; Muse Spark 1.1 described as "a strong encoding model that is very efficient and excels at computer use, tool use, and multimodal understanding," now live through **"our new public API"** and **on OpenRouter for US-based developers**, with distribution ramping "through partner channels and more coding agents over the coming weeks" and enterprise-adoption features in build — i.e. the Muse-API leg MS priced at ~46% incremental ROIC / $30bn revenue per GW (07-27) **is shipping, but management disclosed no API revenue, no token volume and no enterprise logo.** Product slate: **Meta One** subscription ("As demand grows, we're going to offer a variety of different tiers and pricing options there as well"), **Forum** (standalone groups app), **Seller** (standalone marketplace app), Instagram Instance, and **Incognito mode** on WhatsApp and the Meta AI app ("private conversations with their assistant that even Meta can't see"). **Meta Business Agents went globally available on WhatsApp and Messenger this quarter with "more than 1 million businesses using them to talk to their customers or complete sales every week,"** now rolling out on Instagram, plus a new **Meta Business Agent platform** giving enterprises "enterprise-grade controls, guardrails and measurement" — the stated ambition is a **"business in a box service that can help you start and run a whole business using Meta's platforms."** The strategically load-bearing monetisation quote: **"we have a mix of subscriptions, volume-based pricing, and I expect that we're going to continue to evolve more of these products to be like our ad systems where businesses only pay us when we achieve results for them. Over time, that will let us run an efficient auction over our compute."** **Personal agents remain UNSHIPPED** — "Soon, we will have agents that can work 24/7 on your behalf… we're going to have more to share soon," and "there's kind of only so much that I can say on an earnings call about this" — so Nowak's "if no shipped agentic products by Sept, we will be wrong" catalyst (05-08) is still live into Connect (Sept 23). On MSL (Justin Post's question): "I'm quite happy with the trajectory that we're on… we're in the process of scaling much larger and more advanced models"; Muse Spark 1 and 1.1 are "very impressive models **for the scale of the model, the kind of stage of development of the lab**" — an implicit management acknowledgement that they are not yet frontier-scale, consistent with (not contradicting) the model-gap bear on this page. On open source (Sandler/Gawrelski): **"we expect that we will get back to releasing some open source models at some point soon… We plan to do a combination of open and closed models"** — and on whether proliferating open weights could let Meta skip its own frontier effort, **"right now, the open-source models are not as strong as the frontier models, so no is the basic answer,"** with the added strategic case that "having kind of sovereignty over building your own models is going to be an important part of that stack." Zuckerberg's closing bet, worth carrying verbatim for the ROIC debate: **"I get that this is sort of a big bet across the industry. My personal bet is that the people who invest in this are going to be rewarded and feel very good over time."**
- **Cost, headcount, mix and engagement detail net-new to the call (META Q2'26 earnings call, 2026-07-29):** the $42.0B expense line (+55%) was driven by "increases in employee compensation, infrastructure costs, legal-related costs, and **third-party AI token costs**" — **the first time management names external AI token spend as a P&L driver**, confirming as material the Claude-Code/"Claudeconomics" cost line SemiAnalysis flagged 06-30 and FundaAI 06-24. Comp growth ex-severance was "driven by technical hires we've added over the past year, particularly AI talent"; infra growth by "higher depreciation, data center operating costs, and third-party cloud spend." **Excluding the $2.4B legal charge and $1.2B severance, Q2 operating income would have INCREASED 9% y/y** versus the reported −8% — the clean bridge the 8-K did not spell out. **Headcount ended "over 75,000, down 3% from Q1," still including the ~8,000 employees hit by the May-2026 RIF, "the majority of [whom] will no longer be captured in our headcount by the end of Q3 2026"** — i.e. the RIF's run-rate benefit is still ahead, supporting BofA's underappreciated-cost-discipline call (07-20/07-28: May 10% layoff + job postings −49% q/q). Mix: **Family of Apps other revenue crossed $1B for the first time, +73% y/y, on WhatsApp paid messaging and subscriptions**; FoA total revenue $60.4B +28%; FoA ad revenue $59.4B +27% (+26% cc); RL revenue $431M +16% (AI Glasses growth partly offset by lower Quest headset sales). Engagement — this is the "call color pending" arc cell resolved: **Instagram global time spent grew double-digits y/y**; **Facebook video time spent +9% globally and >10% in US & Canada** on ranking improvements; **every public Reels and Feed post on Instagram is now automatically processed through an LLM** across topics-to-tone dimensions, with the Muse family now doing video topic classification/summarisation; the **largest single Reels ranking release to date drove a +15bp increase in sessions on Instagram** (strength in reshares and time spent) and is being brought to Feed with "comparable" early results; **over half of recommended Instagram feed content is now less than one day old, more than double a year ago**; new user controls **"Your Algo"** (Instagram — natural-language prompts to tune recommendations) and **"Shape Your Feed"** (Facebook) with **">80% retention among users who engage with it"**; and a first research milestone continuously pre-training a large-scale model on recommendations data, **"observing healthy scaling laws in the process."** Franchise milestones: **3.6B people use at least one app daily; Instagram reached 2B DAU; Threads crossed 500M MAU ("the fastest-growing conversation app ever"); WhatsApp peaked at 30M messages sent per second during the World Cup final**; Kunal Shah joined as Head of WhatsApp. Hardware: own-brand **Meta Glasses with EssilorLuxottica** (including a Kylie Jenner style), the first to ship with Muse Spark out of the box — **"Early sales have been strong, exceeding our expectations"** — with more at **Connect on September 23**. Legal, unchanged in substance: Li repeated that youth-related US trials scheduled this year "may ultimately result in a material loss."
- **Modular / "tent" datacenter build — the physical mechanism behind the compute-doubling (SemiAnalysis "The Wild Wild West Of LEGO Datacenters", 2026-07-29):** SemiAnalysis notes it "was the first to call out Meta's drastic change to using 'tent' buildings" and that Meta "is standing up fabric-clad 'Tent'-like halls" — at the **Prometheus campus in New Albany** the aluminium-framed, fabric-clad rapid-deployment halls run **~125,000 sq ft each, with eight standing by April 2026** per satellite tracking (announced July 2025), against **two-to-three years for the site's first five permanent buildings** — while cautioning the tents "accelerate the enclosure, not utility interconnection, power, cooling, or commissioning," and trade away durability/long-term flexibility. SemiAnalysis's own reporting and estimates — **not** management commentary; corroborates the "scrappy tent DC design" already on this page from SemiAnalysis 07-09. [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/Ssemianalysis.html)
- **"Meta Compute" launch — strategic pivot from compute buyer to compute seller (Bloomberg, 2026-07-01):** Bloomberg reported Meta is launching **Meta Compute**, an external cloud business selling surplus AI computing capacity to third parties (AWS-Bedrock-like model) — established internally in Jan-2026, with a roadmap to build 10s of GWs by 2030 and hundreds of GWs long-term (Citi · Ronald Josey, 2026-07-01). Stock traded **+6% to +11% intraday** on the headline (up to +9-11% per BofA sales desk; +8.8% single-day per JPM), while SOX/semis fell ~4-6% and memory fell ~11% same session on a reignited capex/AI-demand debate (BofA · Brian Fenske, 2026-07-01; JPM · Ellen Wang, 2026-07-01). This is a major reframing versus the prior "compute-starved pure buyer/renter" narrative carried elsewhere on this page — see Changelog (2026-07-01) for the superseded MS neocloud-optionality framing. Compute-selling build-out: **Prometheus** (1GW+ cluster online '26) and **Hyperion** (5GW+ by '30, initial deployments '28); MTIA chip family (300/400/450/500 generations); **$100B+ in compute-partnership spend over 7+ years** across Google Cloud, CoreWeave, Nvidia, AMD and others (Citi · Ronald Josey, 2026-07-01). Barclays (Ross Sandler, 2026-07-01) flags the read-through risk: Meta turning from a major AI-infra customer into a **competitor for neoclouds** — Meta has signed **$20bn+ TCV deals with CoreWeave and Nebius** plus multi-year deals with Oracle, Google Cloud, AWS; Citi's tech desk (Placeres/Josey/Radke/Rollins/Terry, 2026-07-01) notes AI compute deals currently struck at $40-60bn annually per GW (30-60%+ premium over spot, hyperscaler cash ROIC on AI investment ~29% per Heath Terry), and flags **CoreWeave has ~1/3 of its backlog tied to Meta** — a headline risk if Meta stops signing new neocloud deals in favor of self-supplying/reselling. Corroborating scarcity read: Google has reportedly been limiting Meta's AI/Gemini use due to Google's own capacity constraints (JPM · Wei Zheng Chan desk, 2026-07-01 report, ~3 days after initial report), and META separately signed an agreement with Crusoe (~Jun-18) for ~1.6GW of combined AI computing capacity (JPM · Wei Zheng Chan desk, 2026-07-01) — UBS sales (Christina Dwyer, 2026-07-01) reads the Google-capping-Gemini datapoint as evidence compute remains genuinely scarce, not that Meta has true structural excess to sell.
- **Sell-side reaction to Meta Compute (2026-07-01):** **Citi (Ronald Josey) reiterates Buy, PT $850**, modeling META FY26E/27E capex at **$139B/$171B** and FCF at **$14.6B/$8.5B**; notes recent capacity partnerships valued at ~$50B/GW (Citi, 2026-07-01). **Morgan Stanley (Brian Nowak et al.) reiterates Overweight, PT $775** (unchanged), sizing the neocloud/compute-selling optionality at **$3+ (8%+) of EPS** via monetizing excess capacity; MS estimates META adds ~2GW owned/operated IT capacity in 2026 and ~3.5GW in 2027 (YE25 base ~3GW), plus rents ~2.5GW from third parties (CoreWeave, Nebius, GCP, Oracle) — for comparison MS expects AMZN to add ~5GW and GOOGL ~9GW of IT capacity in 2027; sensitivity: every 250MW leased for 1 year at $40/Watt (~$40B/GW) adds ~$3 (8%) to MS's 2028 EPS; MS capex model assumes **$175bn/$205bn in 2027/2028** (from $145bn in 2026) (MS, 2026-07-01). MS separately flags Meta's Muse model family scored poorly on TerminalBench and SWE-Bench Verified vs Gemini and other frontier models — a gap that matters if Meta wants to be a hosted-API business (MS, 2026-07-01). **JPM (Mark Schilsky desk) buyside color:** 2027E capex estimates relayed across investors range **$175B-$275B** vs Street's $160B, with the majority clustered **$200-225B** — signals very high investor uncertainty on the capex trajectory heading into earnings (JPM, 2026-07-01). (+29% cc); FoA ad revenue $55.0B, +33%. Impressions +19%, price/ad +12% (Q1 FY26 call, Susan Li). Strongest growth among Mag7 ex-GPU sellers.
- **SemiAnalysis "Meta Compute: Everyone Wants To Be A Cloud" (2026-07-02) — the primary bull rebuttal to the overcapacity sell-off:** on the Bloomberg headlines that Meta could become a Neocloud, the market aggressively sold off neoclouds (CoreWeave, [NBIS](NBIS.md)) on "overcapacity" fears — SemiAnalysis argues **both takes are erroneous: Meta's datacenter/compute procurement will ACCELERATE, not slow, and 2027 capex will be "shockingly high."** In just the **first six months of the year Meta contracted over 5GW of capacity across Cloud & Colo** (excl. accelerating self-build); its **two largest campuses alone = 2.5GW under construction**. After nearly **10GW of deals signed since early 2024**, the bulk of capacity adds now come via 3rd parties — so Meta is a huge source of RPO growth for CoreWeave/Nebius, not a demand threat. SemiAnalysis frames **four high-value use-cases** for the compute (differentiated vs traditional neoclouds): (1) **Frontier models — Meta has NOT given up on training; the bulk of incremental capacity still goes to Meta Superintelligence Labs (MSL)**, which SemiAnalysis thinks has a real (if uncertain) shot at catching Anthropic/OpenAI; (2) **RecSys** — Meta believes it can scale ad-recommendation systems >10x in complexity (GEM/HSTU foundation model, ~66% better ranking, 23x effective training FLOPs) and profitably absorb a >10x AdRec compute increase as ROAS grows with CPMs; (3) **(SemiAnalysis exclusive) Meta is in final talks with Anthropic for private Claude instances** — a Bedrock/Foundry/Vertex-style TaaS endpoint, initially own-models external + Anthropic internal, later serving Anthropic/OpenAI externally, leveraging its advertiser base into a Sales & Marketing SaaS; (4) **"SpaceX-type" deals** — at ~$50B/GW annual revenue, allocating just ~200MW externally drives ~$10B/yr at sky-high margin, with 90-day bilateral cancellation letting Meta claw compute back to MSL on short notice (the key tell: if Meta ever signs a deal *without* a SpaceX-style early-cancel clause, "then MSL is actually cooked"). SemiAnalysis expects a "SpaceX-type" deal announced soon — "Anthropic is our prime suspect… a ten billion dollar Anthropic deal to kick off the flywheel." Net: high optionality makes it easy for Meta to keep aggressively contracting compute — "a CFO's dream" (SemiAnalysis "Meta Compute: Everyone Wants To Be A Cloud", 2026-07-02). [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/Semianalysis_META.html)
- **Meta-Compute market reaction — Day 2 (2026-07-02):** the compute-buyer-to-seller pivot triggered a violent factor rotation. **META +8.8%** while **semis −6.3%, neoclouds −8% (CRWV −14%, [NBIS](NBIS.md) −17%), Software +3%** (the degrossing winner), KOSPI −7.9%, "high-beta mo" −9.6% (one of the worst single-day moves, though not a 1-month low) (Rothschild & Co TMT Daily · Rowan Adley, 2026-07-02). Rothschild frames three reads: (i) **Bull / "elegant insurance policy"** — if META rents out 1GW at xAI/Anthropic rates it could generate **~$15bn/yr** and defuse "Metaverse 2.0" anxiety; (ii) **Bear / structural-overcapacity signal** — external leasing = lack of internal ROI / Muse Spark flop, and if META has excess so does the whole ecosystem → capex run-rate cuts coming (starting with META) — the read behind the semis move; (iii) **capital-markets angle** — Zuckerberg may have "planted the story" to pop the stock and then execute a **GOOG-style capital raise to de-risk this year's capex** (Rothschild, 2026-07-02). BofA (Justin Post) keeps **Buy, PO $835** (price $612.91): bull = Meta building a valuable AI-compute asset; bear = external leasing reflects lack of internal-capacity ROI; sees room for multiple re-rating (BofA one-liners · Justin Post, 2026-07-02).
- **Operating income $22.9B, 41% margin.** EPS $10.44 GAAP — but $8.03B one-time tax benefit inflates it; **clean EPS $7.31** (partially relieving the $15.93B '25 R&D-capitalization tax charge) (Q1 FY26 call).
- **Capex $19.8B in Q1; FY26 capex guide RAISED to $125–145B** (from $120–135B), driven mostly by **higher memory/component pricing**, not more gigawatts (Q1 FY26 call; corroborated by Justin Post, BofA, 2026-06-11 equity call). FY26 total opex guide **unchanged $162–169B**; OI expected above FY25. _Prior step-up for reference: 2025 capex guide was raised to ~$71B midpoint (from $69B), up ~$32B YoY, with mgmt flagging "notably larger" dollar growth into 2026 led by infra/cloud/depreciation (Jefferies, 2025-11-10)._
- **Q2 FY26 revenue guide $58–61B** (~2% FX tailwind). Tax rate 13–16% remaining quarters.
- **AI/products:** Muse Spark (first MSL model) shipped, now powering Meta AI across the family + standalone app; double-digit % lift in Meta AI sessions/user. **Muse Spark NOT yet in the ads/recommendation stack** — so the 33% growth is pre-AI-product (emphasized by bull on the BofA call, 2026-06-11). Adaptive ranking (trillion-param-class inference) live; GenAI ad-creative tools at 8M+ advertisers; business AIs 10M weekly convos (up from 1M YTD); value-optimization suite >$20B run-rate. Earlier checkpoint: end-to-end automated ad solutions hit a **$60B annual run-rate** by Q3'25, with ongoing model consolidation (Andromeda retrieval system) driving conversion/quality gains (Jefferies, 2025-11-10). Mgmt has repeatedly framed the core business as **"compute-starved"** — demand outpacing supply — and is staging DC sites + strategic partnerships to preserve option value on future capacity (Jefferies, 2025-11-10).
- **Reality Labs:** Q1 rev $402M (-2%); FY25 op loss tracked ~$4B/qtr. Zuckerberg framed FY25 as **peak RL losses**, pivoting RL spend to glasses/wearables; glasses daily users tripled y/y (Q4 FY25 call, 2026-01-28).
- **Contractual commitments stepped up $107B** in Q1 from multi-year cloud + infra purchase deals (Q1 FY26 call). Headcount 77.9k (-1% q/q); **~10% RIF executed in May** (~8k) per Susan Li / Nowak.
- **Funding gap / off-balance-sheet (JPM "AI Capex 2.0", 2026-06-16):** FY26 capex midpoint raised +$10bn to **$135bn** (mostly memory/component cost); JPM models **~$185bn FY27E / ~$225bn FY28E**. FCF turns negative this year (1Q already $12.4bn FCF vs $19.8bn capex), driving a cumulative **~$97bn funding gap over three years** with the funding strategy "yet to fully reveal itself." Off-income-statement obligations are arguably bigger than the capex: multi-year cloud + infra commitments drove contractual commitments **+$107bn in the quarter to $238bn (+$24bn more in April), alongside $183bn of leases not yet commenced**. META is "the most aggressive hyperscaler in shifting spend off balance sheet," incl. a **20% stake in the Hyperion DC venture backed by ~$28bn of residual-value guarantees**. Returned to the bond market with a **$25bn deal** post-1Q (gross debt → ~$84bn) but said it won't issue debt in consecutive quarters; FT-reported "tens of billions" equity raise was dismissed by mgmt. Backstop: franchise still throws off >$130bn OCF at ~41% op margin (JPM "AI Capex 2.0", 2026-06-16).
- **Digital-ad checks — Meta Business Agents the big swing (UBS Ben Legg + Scott Linzer, 2026-06-17/22):** Legg is increasingly excited about **Meta Business Agents** (formerly Meta Business AI) as the **biggest 5-year tailwind for Meta** — rebranded at a London conference into an "AI business manager" for micro-businesses: a SaaS layer (planned **$15 and $50/month tiers, currently free**) that ingests Meta/Google ads, Stripe, CRM, calendar data + runs ads/WhatsApp on top, plus a "live link when your brand is mentioned in conversations" inventory opportunity (3.5B DAU × 20–30 convos/day). Legg's macro frame: micro-businesses could be ~$1.5T of ad spend by 2030, of which Meta might capture ~$200B; he models a ~1–2pt Q4 tailwind already, lifting Meta ad growth to FY ~30–31% (Q2 34% / Q3 ~28–29% / Q4 ~29%) (UBS Ben Legg, 2026-06-17). Scott Linzer's agency checks are more muted on the core: Meta total Q2 1.4–1.5% / Q3 3.4% / Q4 4.5–5% / FY ~4%, with **Meta pricing flat-to-up (not falling like Google CPCs)** the key hesitation factor, and **Instagram materially underperforming** ("old-school Facebook beating the pants off Instagram"; IG Q2 −2%, FY ~2–2.5%); he calls the **Meta Business AI product "not ready for prime time," underwhelming vs Google, more a 2027 opportunity** (UBS Scott Linzer, 2026-06-22). Note the absolute growth gap between the two panels reflects different client mixes. [Ben Legg](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_06_17_ben_legg_ubs_17_jun_26.html) · [Scott Linzer](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_06_22_scott_linzer_ubs_adv_call_22_jun_26.html)
- **March ad slowdown / 1Q26 print read-through (expert recap + JPM/BofA previews, Apr 2026):** Meta was the only big-three to call out macro — a **March ad-spend slowdown (Middle East, also US/W Europe), recovering in April**; still printed ~33% (~1.5pts below the high-end guide), implying Jan–Feb ran ~35–36%. The first full quarter of EU **less-personalized-ads** regulation exposes ~16% of ad revenue (worst case ~1.5–2pt 2Q hit, in the guide). The ~8k (≈10%) May RIF carries ~$1B severance (gap) for ~$3B annualized savings. Mgmt pushed new product announcements to 2H, removing a 2Q catalyst (expert recap, 2026-04-30). Into the print, BofA expected a beat (Street rev $55.4B, ~70% performance ads, Muse Spark targeted at frontier quality by year-end) and flagged the Reuters 10%-layoff speculation; capex guide raise framed as a "non-event" vs MSFT/GOOG/AMZN, but META still lacks the enterprise/cloud re-rate story (BofA/Fenske, 2026-04-20; JPM, 2026-04-15). [Expert recap](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_04_30_hyper_wrc_recap_30_apr_2026.html) · [BofA preview](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_04_20_fenske_20_apr_26.html) · [JPM preview](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_04_15_jpm_internet_preview_15_apr_26.html)
- **Custom silicon (JPM "AI Capex 2.0", 2026-06-16):** Meta MTIA on a multi-generation roadmap; **Broadcom planning multiple gigawatts of MTIA deployments beginning in 2027**, and Meta intends to train next-gen models on MTIA — corroborates the >1GW own-ASIC ramp.
- **Storage-procurement profile (Former Strategic Procurement Manager at Meta expert call, 2026-01-28):** a rare look at Meta's data-storage supply chain. Unlike AWS/GOOG/MSFT (who buy HDDs as a commodity for cloud-for-customers at the lowest price point), **Meta builds mostly for its own requirement** and balances SSD vs HAMR on TCO. Today Meta's **nearline (warm) storage is ~75-80% HDD / 20-25% SSD (QLC NAND); by 2030 the expert expects ~40% SSD / 60% HDD, on an installed base ~4x today's** (archival/cold storage stays HDD-only). HDD lead times have compressed to ~6-12 months with narrower forecast windows (12-18mo vs prior 3-5yr) as the HDD/SSD substitution and AI demand cloud visibility; Meta over-orders (~1M units vs ~800k needed) to hedge supply, redeploying surplus across sites. Vendor tiering: **WD preferred over Seagate** (higher capacity + NAND partnerships → consolidated SSD+HDD vendor), Toshiba for non-AI storage. QLC SSD priced ~3-4x HDD today, expected to fall to ~2-2.5x by 2028; TLC NAND stays tight/premium 3-4 more years.
