On DRAM, Meta buys LPDDR/DDR5/GDDR for graphics/HPC but views DDR as unattractive for AI (HBM is the mechanism); the expert does not see broad hyperscaler double-ordering as realistic today given how tight/expensive memory is (Former Strategic Procurement Manager at Meta Platforms expert call, 2026-01-28). Corroborates the memory/component-cost driver behind the capex-guide raise.
- **Model gap quantified + Meta IS Anthropic (FundaAI "Deep|LLM 26H1 Part 2", 2026-06-24):** FundaAI's frontier-lab review reframes META's AI position two ways. (1) **Meta is one of Anthropic's largest enterprise customers by token consumption — Claude Code is the company-wide coding standard, NOT its own Muse Spark** — direct evidence the in-house model isn't yet good enough for Meta's own engineers, and a cost line that flows to Anthropic/AWS. Internal "switch back to in-house models / reduce reliance on Anthropic" discussion persists but FundaAI sees near-term implementation as unlikely given the gap. (2) On the models: post-Alexandr-Wang MSL shipped flagship **Muse Spark (codename Avocado, 2026-04-08)** with multimodal understanding "aligned to frontier models but coding clearly behind"; next-gen flagship **Watermelon (3T params)** targets coding catch-up; video model **Mango (vs Sora)** slipped from H1'26 to 2026-09. FundaAI's summary: Meta's flagship "has not reached frontier level in either multimodal or coding." Sharpens the existing bear "model gap" point with current (not Llama-4-era) names (FundaAI, 2026-06-24). [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/LLM_Part_2_funda.html)
- **MetaCode / distillation-risk crackdown (Rothschild & Co TMT Daily, 2026-06-30):** Meta is restricting engineers' use of Anthropic's Claude Code and OpenAI's Codex within its applied-AI engineering division over "distillation" concerns (per The Information); policy in place since May, with Meta capping employee token usage and shifting work to an internal tool ("MetaCode"). Read-through: tension between the SemiAnalysis finding (2026-06-30, above) that Meta is only a 3-5% customer for Anthropic and this policy — Meta is trying to wean off external frontier-model dependence even as its own models (Muse Spark) remain "clearly behind" on coding (FundaAI, 2026-06-24).
- **Pre-Meta-Compute bear overhang (JPM · Mark Schilsky desk, 2026-06-30):** ahead of the Meta Compute news, JPM's desk flagged ROIC on Zuckerberg's "Superintelligence Lab" spend as currently seen by investors as **"highly negative,"** with uncertainty on whether the AI effort becomes a Reels-like product cycle or a Metaverse-like miss; also flagged a **"significant equity issuance overhang"** post-GOOGL's recent capital raise. (Superseded/reframed one day later by the Meta Compute launch — see Current-state bullet above and Changelog 2026-07-01.)
- **Barclays "AI's S-Curve Is Steepening" (2026-06-01):** includes META in its hyperscaler-capex aggregate (GOOGL/AWS/META/MSFT/ORCL) that Barclays now sees reaching **~$1.16T by 2028E** (25%+ above Street), as the AI capex peak gets pushed beyond 2028 (~36 GW of next-gen compute); META carried **OW/Positive, PT not changed in this note** ($635 ref) — incidental inclusion in the capex framework, no META-specific thesis change (Barclays, 2026-06-01).
- **'Watermelon' next-gen model + 'Pocket' vibe-coding app (MS TMT Sales · Thomas Wigg, 2026-07-06):** Alexandr Wang says META's next-gen model **'Watermelon' matches GPT-5.5 capabilities** and uses **"an order of magnitude" more compute** than the prior 'Avocado' model (BI, via MS TMT 2026-07-06); reportedly on par with GPT-5.5 (Bernstein TMT, 2026-07-06). META also **soft-launched a vibe-coding gaming app 'Pocket'** (MS TMT · Thomas Wigg, 2026-07-06). Read-through: sharpens the "model gap" debate — the in-house flagship (Watermelon = the 3T-param next-gen per FundaAI 2026-06-24, above) is now claimed at GPT-5.5 parity, a direct partial rebuttal to the "clearly behind on coding" bear point; the order-of-magnitude compute step also corroborates the aggressive capex/compute-buildout trajectory. (MS TMT Sales · Thomas Wigg, 2026-07-06)
- **Internet-capex/capacity/monetization deep-dive — market giving Meta "little to negative value" on its capacity build (BofA "Raising Internet capex ests", 2026-07-07):** BofA (Justin Post) raises Meta capex to **$145bn 2026 (vs $130bn prior) / $185bn 2027 (vs $157bn prior)** (2028 ~$210bn). In the GW analysis Meta has the **highest cost per incremental GW of the Big-3 — ~$45bn/GW in 2026 (vs Google $37bn, Amazon $25bn)** — reflecting greater upfront land/building spend ahead of GPU deployment, reliance on external GPU infrastructure, and more specialized AI (vs core-cloud) capacity. BofA sizes Meta's long-run optionality: **up to ~23GW of capacity by 2030, and if 40% is available for AI enterprise sales → a ~$110bn potential enterprise opportunity** (at a conservative $12bn/GW, within the $10-17bn/GW range BofA models for AWS/Google Cloud). The valuation punchline is the bear-relevant one: backing out Meta's ad business at a conservative 5x 2027 revenue (~12x P/E) and assuming 60% of capacity serves the core ad business, the Street is embedding only **~$4bn per AI GW** — "little to negative value on the capacity build," vs Google ~$110bn and Amazon ~$59bn per GW. BofA reads this as the largest implied discount in the group and the source of upside if Meta can prove capacity monetization (via Meta Compute / enterprise sales) — recent Anthropic/Google-SpaceX deals at **>$40bn/GW (up to ~$50bn/GW)** are the proof-point. **Reiterate Buy, PO $835** (BofA "Raising Internet capex ests", 2026-07-07). [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/Bofa_on_Capex.html)
- **Neocloud read — "excess demand, not excess supply" (MS Semis Weekly · Joseph Moore, 2026-07-06):** on the Bloomberg (2026-07-01) report that META is building a cloud business to sell excess AI compute — which the market (mis)read as a sign of excess capacity (Bitcoin miners −20%, Global Memory −15%, Broad AI −7.5% on the week) — MS/Joe Moore frames it as **"excess demand, not excess supply"**: GPU compute remains in shortage and subletting GPUs is a positive for NVDA share, not a demand-cut signal. Corroborating scarcity read: GOOGL reportedly told META it could **NOT offer all the Gemini capacity META wanted to buy** (FT, via MS 2026-07-06). (MS Semis Weekly · Joseph Moore, 2026-07-06)
- **SemiAnalysis "The Future of Meta Superintelligence: A 1 Year Progress Update" (2026-07-09) — the bull case on MSL's *trajectory* ("what matters is the slope, not the intercept"):** SemiAnalysis argues Meta is the **only hyperscaler/neolab on track to be world-class at all three of data, talent and compute**, giving it the best shot at catching Anthropic/OpenAI for the #3 frontier spot ("the race for 3rd is currently between Meta and SpaceX, not Google"). **DATA (newest, most underappreciated edge):** Meta reassigned **~3,000 engineers** (incl. ~70% of new grads + many seniors) to full-time RL task/environment creation — a new "applied AI engineering org" announced late-May — and began **tracking employees' screens/keyboard/mouse** to capture in-house white-collar-workflow data; its RL-hours are already in the same ballpark as **Mercor's 2,517,000 expert hours logged in 2Q26 (~4,800 FTEs)**, with ~70k more people to pull from. **COMPUTE:** SemiAnalysis's **Tokenomics Model projects Meta will have more AI compute than both OpenAI and Anthropic by end-2026**; even conservatively assigning only specific DC sites to MSL, Meta's training compute is comparable to OpenAI/Anthropic through 2026-27 (Zuck willing to run FCF-negative). **DATACENTER:** five simultaneous 1GW+ "titan" clusters (Prometheus ~1GW→>3GW; Hyperion ~1.5GW under construction incl. 3×400MW world-largest buildings; El Paso, Iowa 1GW lease, Indiana), scrappy "tent" DC design, up to **2,000km scale-across** via "AI-Backbone (AIBB)" (~22 petabits/s) — full detail on [ai-datacenter-power theme](themes/ai-datacenter-power.md). **MODEL:** **Muse Spark** (Apr-2026) lagged DeepSeek v4 Pro and Kimi K2.6 on most benchmarks at release; **Muse Spark 1.1** tested ~on par with Opus 4.6 / GLM 5.2 (priced just under GLM 5.2), but SemiAnalysis is **moving none of its own token volume to it yet** and doesn't expect Anthropic/OpenAI parity until end-2026. **TALENT:** Andrew Tulloch (ex-Thinking Machines cofounder) + TM founding team (Joshua Gross/Mark Jen/Yinghai Lu); ex-OpenAI Jason Wei/Hyung Won Chung/Zhiqing Sun; **Dina Powell McCormick** as President & Vice Chairman (compute buildout); and OpenAI's compute trio (Hoeschele/Saharan/Hemani, one already quit citing infra-org culture). **What would turn SemiAnalysis bearish:** Meta signing a long-term compute-sale deal **with no clawback**, disbanding the RL task-creation org, or letting top researchers walk = a potential "death sentence" for MSL (ties to the SpaceX-style 90-day-cancel-clause "tell" from the 07-02 Meta Compute piece). This is a SemiAnalysis opinion/estimate piece — the Tokenomics/compute projections are SemiAnalysis's own. (SemiAnalysis "The Future of Meta Superintelligence", 2026-07-09) [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/Semianalysis_META.html)
- **Broadcom-side read — Meta a top-5 frontier builder + MTIA-resale optionality (JPMorgan · Harlan Sur "Hock Tan @ JPM", 2026-07-07):** on the JPM-hosted Broadcom call, Hock named **Meta among the ~5 frontier-model developers** (Llama, "trying to catch up…still investing") that together drive ~80% of compute demand, and confirmed Meta as a **Broadcom ASIC customer (MTIA)**. Asked about the "Meta Compute" rumor, Hock said there is **no reason Meta couldn't sell its MTIA chips to other frontier-model developers** — the same playbook as Google renting TPUs to Anthropic — because Meta has built a **full PyTorch software stack** ("totally open"). He also **dismissed the "oversupply of compute" concern for 2027/28** ("I don't think there's over supply…far from it"). A supplier-side validation of both the Meta-Compute-as-merchant-cloud thesis and the demand-durability (no-overcapacity) rebuttal already on this page. Iris is aimed at improving AI for Facebook/Instagram, with **[AVGO](AVGO.md) (Broadcom) as chief design partner and [TSM](TSM.md) (TSMC) manufacturing Iris**. Meta signed multiple long-term supply agreements — **Samsung (memory), [SNDK](SNDK.md) (SanDisk, flash storage), Sumitomo (fiber optic)** — and plans to launch a new AI processor every ~6 months. Concrete date + supply-chain detail on the in-house-ASIC ramp, corroborating the >1GW / multi-GW-from-2027 MTIA framing and refining the 07-10 @ReutersBiz "manufacturing own chip from September / 14GW next year" datapoint with the 7GW-2026 base and named suppliers. But **Meta lacks an enterprise sales/service motion**, so he expects **opportunistic XAI-style deals with a few big whales (potentially other hyperscalers)**, not a scaled cloud; he'd have Meta buy a front-end like Databricks (Meta is now an investor). On models, **Muse Spark 1.1 "looks quite good"** but still short of the leading Chinese/proprietary stacks. Reinforces the "Meta Compute is opportunistic capacity monetization, not a GCP-scale business" reading. [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_07_13_archera_13_jul_26.html)

Cross-cutting capital/leverage read-through: **@Hedgeye (2026-07-13)** notes Google, Amazon, Microsoft, Meta and Oracle have piled up **nearly $700B in debt** as AI-datacenter spending surges — a datapoint feeding the financing/equity-raise debate already on this page. (@firstadopter citing CNBC; @FundaAI; @Hedgeye, 2026-07-13)
- **Muse Spark 1.1 benchmark sweep + low-price go-to-market (2026-07-13/14):** Alexandr Wang posted a run of Muse Spark 1.1 results that further narrow the "model clearly behind" bear — **#3 on the Debate Benchmark (behind only Fable 5 and Opus 4.7, ahead of GPT-5.6 Sol)**, **SOTA on RadLE-H (radiology)**, **SOTA on HealthBench Professional** ("best health model out there"), and a **perfect score on the 2026 Asian Physics Olympiad** (corroborated internally by @shengjia_zhao) (@alexandr_wang, 2026-07-13/14). These are first-party benchmarks; independent confirmation still pending, but the cadence sharpens the model-parity debate. (@alexandr_wang; The Information, 2026-07-13/14)

Packaging EMIB-T/CoPoS Ramps", 2026-08-16).** **"VR200 NVL72: production on track for a SepQ launch and DecQ volume ramp, potentially 2.5–3K NVL72 RACKS IN 2026E — driven by META AND xAI."** ➜ A supply-chain confirmation that Meta's 2026 capacity adds are landing on the newest [[NVDA]] platform rather than on trailing-generation parts, which is the opposite of the "Plan B on H100s" characterisation MS applied to the *rentable* fleet (08-04, on this page) — the two are compatible (new racks for own workloads, older silicon for any merchant capacity) but the distinction should be kept explicit. ⚠️ **Mizuho carries META at OUTPERFORM (price $589.85 in the note's table) and publishes NO price target for it here — do not infer one.** Context from the same note, relevant to the capex debate on this page: **2027E >50K VR racks and >20K GB**, with NVDA "limited by CoWoS and DC power," and **CSP capex +111%/+50% in 2026E/2027E to ~$1T/~$1.5T**. ## Capstone estimates (house model)
_Source: Capstone official model (`Modelo Meta pós 2Q26.xlsm`, 2026-06-11)._

| | 2025E | 2026E | 2027E |
|---|--:|--:|--:|
| Revenue ($bn) | 201 | 257 | 313 |
| &nbsp;&nbsp;— y/y | — | +28% | +22% |
| EPS ($) | 29.06 | 32.72 | 38.24 |
| EBIT margin | ~42% | ~37% | ~35% |
| Capex ($bn) | 70 | 131 | 170 |
| FCF ($bn) | 22 | -17 | -23 |

**Scenario PT:** Bear **$525** / Base **$688** / Bull **$840** (EPS 35 / 38 / 42 × 15 / 18 / 20x).
**House read:** revenue $201 → $313bn (25-27E); capex doubles to $170bn pushing FCF negative; base PT $688 at ~18× EPS '27. (Capstone official model, 2026-06-11)

## Debate / thesis
- **Citrini “PEMDAS” bull case — monetize compute through ads before requiring a cloud/model payoff (2026-07-20):** Meta’s order of operations is compute → better models/retrieval/creative → higher advertiser returns and revenue → reinvested compute. Citrini cites Meta hardware/software co-design producing a **10,000× increase in Andromeda model capacity, 10× inference-efficiency improvement, >3× query throughput, 6% retrieval-recall improvement and 8% ad-quality improvement in selected segments**; Advantage+ shopping campaigns delivered a **22% advertiser-ROAS increase**, while image-generation features drove ~**7% conversion lift**. This is the cleanest answer to the capex/ROIC bear because the monetization path already sits inside the advertising P&L; frontier models, enterprise APIs and external inventory are optionality (Citrini Semis, “All Along the AI Watchtower,” 2026-07-20). On the model economics, Citrini also flags **Meta Muse blended token pricing at ~$1** (“90% cost savings vs Anthropic,” undercutting even Grok) — sharpening the aggressive-low-pricing GTM already tracked from JEF/Blayne (07-10) and MS (Muse API 30-80% below the private labs, 07-14) (Citrini Semis, 2026-07-20).
- **CTV/open-web extension:** Citrini frames Meta as a potential demand/optimization layer over third-party inventory rather than a media owner, citing **$45.2B of US CTV/online-video advertiser spend in 2025** and **~$42.5B of open-web programmatic display by 2027**. The edge is the combination of advertiser demand, generated creative, retrieval, optimization, measurement and closed-loop conversion data (Citrini Semis, 2026-07-20).
- **Octahedron Capital new position (FUND, 2026-07-15):** Octahedron established a **new 5-10% META position** (alongside a new MSFT position) at its 2Q26 LP call — the ROIC on AI for platforms **without a built-in hyperscaler is "highly positive,"** revenue growth can run **"well north of 20%"** on engagement + ad units/pricing, with **optionality if Meta builds a hyperscaler (80% gross margins)**; the fund says Meta "came out with a pretty good model." A fresh buy-side conviction datapoint on the same ROIC/monetization axis the sell-side bears contest (Octahedron 2Q26 LP call, 2026-07-15).
- **07-20 hyperscaler-cross-note flow (JPM credit + BofA setup):** into the print, **JPM (Anmuth/Chatterjee/Speer) DOWNGRADED META credit overweight→neutral** in its "Hyperscalers Joint Equity & Credit call" — modeling **+42% capex to ~$200bn**, ~$25bn of USD debt, a stance of not issuing debt in back-to-back quarters (next window Nov/Feb), a **1x net-leverage trigger**, and META reportedly leasing compute to Anthropic while floating external compute sales (JPM, 2026-07-20). **BofA (Post) stays bullish (demanda/bull), PO $835 (24x):** rev ~$61bn (street), EPS upside on layoffs/lower SBC; Silver Torch / new Spark re-architecture; external compute-sales optionality (7GW next year per Reuters; monetize $10-40bn/GW; BofA placeholder **$5bn '27 / $11bn '28**; leasing to Anthropic); **SOTP ~$600 core + ~$250 capacity** (BofA "Big Tech setups lightning round", 2026-07-20).
- **"Meta Compute" — the compute-buyer-to-seller pivot (2026-07-01):** the single biggest thesis development since the last print. Bloomberg confirmed Meta is launching an external AI-cloud business ("Meta Compute," AWS-Bedrock-like) to sell surplus capacity to third parties — directly answering the long-standing bear/bull debate point on this page that META "lacks the enterprise/cloud re-rate vehicle" (Wigg/Thill, 2026-06-01/05-06, below) and converts MS's prior "backup optionality" framing (~$10B/GW, 2026-05-08) into an active, confirmed business line, now sized by MS at **$40/Watt (~$40B/GW)** with **$3+ (8%+) of 2028 EPS** upside (MS · Nowak, 2026-07-01). **Bull framing:** Citi (Josey) reiterates Buy $850 PT on the news, modeling FY26E/27E capex $139B/$171B and FCF $14.6B/$8.5B, with capacity partnerships valued ~$50B/GW (Citi, 2026-07-01) — validates the capex spend as monetizable rather than a pure cost center. **Bear/complication framing:** Barclays (Ross Sandler, 2026-07-01) flags this turns Meta from a **major neocloud customer into a neocloud competitor** — Meta has $20bn+ TCV deals with CoreWeave/Nebius plus multi-year Oracle/GCP/AWS deals; CoreWeave alone has ~1/3 of its backlog tied to Meta (Citi tech desk, 2026-07-01), so a pivot to self-supply/resell is a headline risk to those neocloud names. UBS sales (Christina Dwyer, 2026-07-01) counters that Google simultaneously capping Meta's Gemini access on Google's own capacity constraints argues compute is still genuinely scarce, i.e. Meta's "excess" may be thinner than the market's +6-11% stock reaction implies. JPM's buyside color (Schilsky desk, 2026-07-01) shows the market has NOT converged on a 2027 capex number — investor estimates range $175B-$275B (cluster $200-225B) vs Street's $160B — meaning Meta Compute raises the capex debate's stakes rather than resolving it. MS also flags a **model-quality gap** (Muse scored poorly on TerminalBench/SWE-Bench Verified vs Gemini) as a risk to the hosted-API ambition (MS, 2026-07-01).
- **Neocloud debate — "excess demand, not excess supply" (MS · Joseph Moore / Brian Nowak, 2026-07-06):** the newest sell-side read on the Meta-Compute pivot cuts against the overcapacity bear. **Bull (demand-side):** MS/Joe Moore reads the plan to sell excess compute as evidence of "excess demand, not excess supply" — GPU compute is still in shortage, so subletting is a positive (for NVDA share too), not a signal META is over-built; the FT datapoint that GOOGL couldn't supply all the Gemini capacity META asked for reinforces genuine scarcity (MS Semis Weekly · Joseph Moore, 2026-07-06). **Bull (capital/optionality):** MS/Brian Nowak stays bullish on the neocloud optionality — **~$3+/~8% EPS upside** from leasing capacity in a capacity-constrained world — while stressing the **core OW thesis remains product innovation (MetaAI, agents, subscriptions), NOT becoming a neocloud** (MS · Brian Nowak, 2026-07-06). This re-frames the leasing story as upside insurance rather than the thesis, and is consistent with (does not supersede) the 07-01 MS $40/Watt / $3+/8% sizing already carried above. The bear counter (overcapacity → capex-cut read-through; market sold Bitcoin miners −20% / Global Memory −15% / Broad AI −7.5% on the week per MS 2026-07-06) is the debate this reframing pushes back on.
- **Capex trajectory reaffirmed (MS · Brian Nowak, 2026-07-06):** MS models META capex rising to **$145B in 2026, $175B in 2027, $205B in 2028** — the same path as the 07-01 note (no change; reaffirmed). SemiAnalysis (2026-07-06) expects META capex to accelerate "shockingly high" into 2027: META has contracted **>5GW of cloud+colo capacity in 1H26** and signed **~10GW of deals since early 2024**, and will remain a large 3rd-party cloud/colo customer (RPO growth for neoclouds, not replacing them). FUNDA read: META is reallocating older-gen NVDA Hopper GPUs to inference/external monetization while reserving Blackwell/Rubin for frontier training — i.e. the "excess compute" being sold is legacy silicon, not next-gen capacity being idled. (MS · Brian Nowak; SemiAnalysis; FUNDA, all 2026-07-06)
- **MS raises capex again — META top pick, "$1.4trln of hyperscaler capex by '28" (MS · Brian Nowak, 2026-07-12):** MS's cross-hyperscaler deep-dive raises **META '27/'28 capex by 29%/22% to $225bn/$250bn** (from the $175bn/$205bn reaffirmed 07-06 — see Changelog for the superseded figure), on a ~20% higher bottom-up cost-per-GW (memory + powered-shell inflation) and rising forward capacity. MS keeps **META as its top pick with 5 under-appreciated call options**, and sees **META reaching 14/21 total GW of capacity by '27/'28** (from ~3.5 GW at YE25). Framed inside the sector call: total hyperscaler capex (incl. SPCX for the first time) now **~$1.2trln/$1.4trln in '27/'28** (+9%/+10%), driving a **4x lift in available compute to ~120 GW by '28 (from ~30 in '25)**; 3-year build timelines + social/political backlash pull spending forward, adding to inflation. (MS · Brian Nowak et al. "Internet: $1.4trln of Capex…", 2026-07-12) [also [[hyperscaler-capex]]] **Buyside triangulation (BofA bogey poll, 07-12):** the buyside-avg FY27 capex bogey is **$204.2B** (FY26 $147.1B / FY28 $248.6B / FY29 $235.8B), and the freshest post-'14GW-memo' conversations are running **$215-230B** for 2027 — materially above BofA analyst Justin Post's **maintained $185B** estimate, the freshest signal on where buyside capex expectations have settled (BofA · Justin Post / Fenske, 2026-07-12). Cross-name context: BofA notes Big-5 consensus 2026-2028 capex-as-%-of-revenue was **~37.5% mid-April** (up from ~11% end-'24); plugging in the buyside capex bogeys lifts that intensity to **~46.5%** (BofA · Brian Fenske, 2026-07-12).
- **Best week in >2 years (Jefferies Tech · Favuzza, 2026-07-12):** META had its **best week in >2 years (+15%)**, the top single-stock gainer in Internet, on hype around **Muse Spark 1.1** + incremental comfort on AI ROI — the tape confirmation of the sentiment inflection flagged 07-10/11. (Jefferies Tech · Jeffrey Favuzza, 2026-07-12)
- **Overcapacity bear inverts — "2x compute / ~14GW / ~$220B 2027" (Bernstein · Tyler Seidman, 2026-07-08/09):** one week after the market's overcapacity scare, Reuters reported META plans to **~2x compute capacity to ~14GW next year**, with Bernstein's Seidman increasingly hearing **total 2027 capex ~$220B** — the newest up-escalation and corroboration of the existing capex-up thesis (BofA $185bn'27 / MS $175bn'27 already on the page; @mvcinvesting frames the reversal bluntly: "one week ago the market feared excess compute; today Reuters reports META plans to double compute"). Seidman's earlier read (07-08) had already framed **Muse Image** positively (META progressing toward a competitive frontier lab) while noting image quality implies AI agents must still improve to justify current capex, with Bernstein's 2027 hyperscaler-capex shape increasingly **~+50% YoY (≈ +$220B for META)**. Seidman attributes META's late-day drop 07-08 to the **Grok 4.5 release + a $10B/1GW Canada data-center investment** landing simultaneously, and flags renewed discussion of whether META's **reported (FT) equity raise is imminent** — reopening the financing overhang the Meta-Compute story had partly quieted. **MS TMT (Wigg, 07-08): "META says high capex intact"** (management reaffirming plans). **BofA added META to its US 1 (best-ideas) list (US 1 Committee, 2026-07-09)** — a positive endorsement alongside BofA's standing Buy/$835 PO. Model-gap read-through: JEF TMT (Rankie Wong) / @alexeheath (via @jukan05, 07-08) say **Meta AI expects to surpass Opus-level performance this year** with the large frontier model in training — a further datapoint (alongside the 07-06 'Watermelon' GPT-5.5-parity claim) narrowing the "model clearly behind" bear. Minor offset: JPM's Chips-for-Breakfast credit desk flagged **IG downgrades of MSFT/META** (07-09, credit, minor). CTO Bosworth (via @SouthernValue95, 07-08) tempered the scaling narrative: raw intelligence keeps scaling with compute, "but human tasks don't have infinite headroom."

Crisafulli places the market's frontier-AI pecking order as **Anthropic, OpenAI, Meta, Google, SpaceX** — i.e. Meta now perceived at the #3 frontier spot (echoing SemiAnalysis's "race for 3rd is Meta vs SpaceX" framing, 07-09 above). Catalysts: ship a "Meta Claw"/Muse-powered personal+shoppable agent (not priced; stock at ~15-16x is "saying he's burning money in a hole"); ad rev can stay high-20s and grow ~20-25% in '27 on core algo improvements alone before any AI product. Neocloud "backup optionality" = leasing excess capacity at ~$10B/GW = 8%+ upside to '28 EPS (MS note, briefing 2026-05-18). The MS hyperscaler-capex bull: convert capex to revenue/margin and you get EPS upside + a multiple re-rate. **BofA (Justin Post) Reit. Buy, PT $835** (morning briefing 2026-06-16). **Barclays OW, PT $770** (raised through the year; valuation = avg of 25x EPS and 12x EBITDA on FY27E; PT path $640 Apr → $810 Jul → $770 Oct'25) (Barclays, 2025-11-17).
- **New page-high, and the most out-of-consensus capex forecast on the page — R&Co Redburn (Dom Ball) Buy, PT $1,000, on capex of ~$145bn in BOTH 2026 and 2027 falling toward ~$100bn in 2028 (Rothschild & Co Redburn · Dom Ball, "One Prompt Entrepreneur," 135pp, via Rowan Joseph's "Redburn 5," 2026-07-26):** the analyst who had been the page's long-standing **Shopify bull has downgraded SHOP (Buy → Neutral, PT $160 → $130) and moved the money to META** — a paired trade, which is what makes the call legible. **PT $1,000 = 17x ~$60 of 2028 EPS versus Street "closer to $40"** (68% upside cited at the time of the note). This is now the highest mark on the page (prior high: Citi Buy $850; BofA Buy $835). **The thesis: META pivots "from a mediocre consumer LLM to an end-to-end SMB AI platform"** via a "one prompt entrepreneur" tool — a merchant photographs a product, picks a target market, and Meta builds the storefront, catalogue, pricing, creative and CRM, feeding the data and advertising straight into Advantage+. Three revenue pools: software subscriptions; **first-party intent and transaction data (framed as reversing Apple's ATT damage)**; and incremental SMB formation driving ad demand. Sizing: **~$80bn of SMB-LLM revenue by 2030 on 30% penetration of Meta's 250m on-platform businesses**, with total "other revenue" (incl. AI cloud) approaching **$139bn**; the bundle is argued to deliver **~25% higher ROAS than standalone alternatives**. On regulation, Ball inverts the usual read — META as the **"modern-day Philip Morris,"** where tighter rules raise barriers and protect the incumbent, citing **EU advertising growth accelerating ~500bps FX-neutral in the quarter after Europe's December-2025 rules** and Australia's under-16 ban affecting **<1% of revenue**. **⚠️ Two things to flag rather than smooth over.** First, a **codename collision this page should not resolve by fiat: Redburn calls the SMB tool "Watermelon," while this page already carries "Watermelon" as META's next-gen *frontier* model** (GPT-5.5 parity, ~10x the compute of "Avocado" — MS TMT/Wigg + Bernstein, 2026-07-06), and UBS's 07-26 desk note also lists "Muse + Watermelon (frontier model)." Either Redburn is applying the frontier-model codename to a product built on it, or one of the two attributions is wrong — logged as an open discrepancy. Second, and more consequential for the model: **Redburn's ~$145bn 2027 capex is the low outlier on this page by a wide margin** — versus buy-side bogeys of **$204.2bn (BofA poll)** and **$225-250bn (UBS desk)**, Bernstein's ~$220bn, JPM's ~$200-202bn, MS's $175bn and the house's own $170bn — and its **2028 capex *declining* to ~$100bn contradicts every other forecast on the page**, including BofA's buy-side bogey of $248.6bn. Ball reaches an FCF yield of 1% → 3% on that path. The PT is therefore best read as **conditional on a capex trajectory nobody else models**; the SMB-LLM revenue thesis and the capex call should be judged separately. Filed to [wiki-edge](_meta/edge.md) as an open divergence. (R&Co Redburn, 2026-07-26)
  - 🔴 **PRIMARY RECEIVED 2026-08-29 — THE 135-PAGE PDF ITSELF, AND IT CORRECTS THE RELAY ON THE TWO NUMBERS THAT MATTER MOST. The bullet above was built from Rowan Joseph's "Redburn 5" relay (2026-07-26); the note is dated 21 JULY 2026 and is authored by Dominic Ball (w/ Joseph Barker, Zeshan Shiekh).** ⚠️ **(1) THE 2028 CAPEX FIGURE WAS WRONG BY ~30%: the relay said capex "falling toward ~$100bn in 2028"; the primary's table says $131,377m — i.e. $131.4bn, versus consensus $184.0bn (−29%). The direction of the call survives intact, the magnitude does not. 2026e/2027e are $141,571m/$142,156m (the relay's "~$145bn in both years" was close but not exact).** ⚠️ **(2) THE PT BRIDGE IS A 2027 BRIDGE, NOT A 2028 ONE: the note states the upgrade as *"we upgrade our Meta (Buy) PT to $1,000 (2027 GAAP EPS $43.2 AT 23x) FROM $900."* The relay's "17x ~$60 of 2028 EPS" arrives at the same $1,000 by a different route (RDBN 2028 EPS is indeed $59.0, and $1,000/59.0 = 16.9x), so both are arithmetically defensible — but the house's own stated basis is 23x 2027, and that is what should be quoted.** ✅ **Confirmed unchanged by the primary: the paired trade ([[SHOP]] Buy → Neutral, PT $160 → $130), ~$80bn of SMB-LLM revenue by 2030 on 30% penetration of c250m on-platform businesses, the c$209bn ex-China TAM, the Apple-ATT-arbitrage leg sized at $23bn, the "modern-day Philip Morris" regulation inversion (PM outperformed the S&P by >1,000bp p.a. from the 1960s to 2000), and Street 2028 EPS "closer to $40" (primary: consensus $40.33).** **New from the primary: RDBN forecasts a 45% IRR for the SMB LLM — above both core ads and any AI-cloud route — and puts the SMB-LLM revenue ramp at c$2bn → $80bn cumulative across 2026-2030 at an average $78/month per adopting SMB.** ➤ **The "Watermelon" codename collision flagged above is NOT resolved by the primary: the 135pp note calls the model MUSE SPARK (*"Meta's latest closed-source LLM, built by the Meta Superintelligence Labs (MSL) team, led by Alexandr Wang"*) and does not use "Watermelon" for the SMB tool at all — which points to the RELAY, not Redburn, as the source of the collision. Open discrepancy narrowed, not closed.** (Rothschild & Co Redburn · Dominic Ball, "One Prompt Entrepreneur," 135pp, **2026-07-21**) [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/One_Prompt_Entrepreneur_1.html)
- **Robotics/wearables optionality (Brian Nowak, MS — OW, PT $615 at time of note; "Humanoids"):** META laying the foundation for a robotics offering — PARTNR program (human-robot interaction benchmark/dataset for the home) + FAIR embodied-AI work. MS sees a wearables install base path to **15mn+ next year**; the same software/sensors/compute stack feeds robotics/humanoids. Underappreciated angle: the **"off-line world data"** captured by wearables/robotics (everything seen/heard) is a moat for training future differentiated models — MS argues this is not in the price even though META is the "cleanest GenAI winner."
- **"Meta wrapper / Zuck Cloud" call option (Jefferies conf, 2026-06-01):** a Jefferies CIO said he would bring Meta into his enterprise environment **if Meta launched a commercial AI-model wrapper/cloud** (Red Hat-style support/maintenance layer around its open models, à la AWS/Azure/GCP) — framed as an under-priced bull optionality: Meta has the model + 3.5B-user distribution but no enterprise/cloud monetization vehicle yet, the absence of which is exactly why it doesn't get a GOOG-style re-rate (Jefferies/Brent Thill conf, 2026-06-01). Consistent with the BofA spec-sales bull view that META screened as the **cheapest Mag7 on relative forward PE** post the social-media-litigation selloff, with the best post-earnings revisions of the group and a latent consumer-AI opportunity across IG/WhatsApp/FB (BofA/Fenske, 2026-04-16); BofA/Wigg counters it remains a **"show-me story"** needing a "wow" shipped product to re-rate vs the easier capex-justification for GOOG/MSFT/AMZN (BofA/Wigg, 2026-05-06). Ad-agency read-through: digital spend is a "tale of two cities" — efficiency cleanup everywhere funding continued scaling into the Google/Meta/TikTok/Amazon **walled gardens at everyone else's expense** (UBS Vayner Media, 2026-06-18). [Jefferies conf](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_06_01_brent_jeff_ai_1_jun_26.html) · [Fenske](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_04_16_fenske_16_apr_26.html) · [Wigg](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_05_06_wigg_6_may_26.html) · [Vayner](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_06_18_ubs_vayner_media_18_jun_26.html)
- **Ad-tech / macro framing (Eric Seufert, Stratechery interview, 2025-04-24):** META's largest advertiser segment is e-commerce/DTC, much of it China-sourced — the one place META is "absolutely exposed" to broad tariffs (ROAS down → budgets down), beyond the over-hyped Temu/Shein risk. But Seufert's net read is structurally bullish: in a downturn there's a **flight-to-quality** that consolidates spend onto META (large advertisers diversify on principle but META takes share from small platforms). On the black box: META's Andromeda retrieval + Advantage+ make it the leader; he believes META is "slow-rolling" full ad automation (could go further but doesn't want to spook advertisers) — i.e. latent monetization upside.
- 📚🔴🔴 **HISTORICAL BACKFILL, DATED 2026-06-11 — THE BofA "FEEDS TO FRONTIER MODELS" DEEP-DIVE, AND ITS ONE DURABLE CONTRIBUTION IS THE ONLY FULLY-SPECIFIED *DEPRECIATION-OFFSET* ARITHMETIC ON THIS PAGE: HOW MUCH INCREMENTAL REVENUE META MUST GENERATE IN 2029 TO PAY FOR THE 2025-28 AI CAPEX, SOLVED THREE WAYS.** (BofA Securities · **Justin Post** w/ Nitin Bansal, *"Meta Platforms Inc: From Feeds to Frontier Models: Meta's Structural Evolution in the AI era"*, **2026-06-11**, 30pp.) [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/20260611_BofA_Securities_META_Meta_Platforms_Inc-_From_Feeds_to_Frontier_Models-_Meta.html)
  ⚠️⚠️ **STALENESS — READ THIS BEFORE QUOTING ANYTHING BELOW. Ingested 2026-09-01 from a THREE-MONTH-OLD note. Its rating/PT (**BUY, PO $835, established 30-Apr-2026, price $570.98**) is **SUPERSEDED** — see `## Changelog`. Its price, multiple and 2026 capex marks are dead. It gets NO row in the current `## Intra-quarter` block because it PREDATES the window (which opened at the 2026-07-29 print); it sits here as dated historical context only. **What survives is the FRAMEWORK, not the levels.**
  - **THE SETUP IT WAS WRITTEN INTO, which is worth preserving because it dates the sentiment trough this page has been arguing about ever since: *"Following the 3Q25 earnings call, when Meta guided to a material step-up in both 2026 Opex and Capex, the stock has underperformed broader indices (**Meta -24% vs. NASDAQ +5%**). Investor concerns have centered on a shift to a more capital-intensive business model (lower FCF), uncertain ROI of AI-related spend, and future impact from D&A on margins."*** ➤ **The same three concerns are still the spine of the bear case on this page in September. The note is the cleanest dated statement of when they were first priced.**
  - 🔴🔴 **① THE BASELINE TRICK THAT MAKES THE WHOLE THING WORK, AND IT IS REUSABLE ON ANY HYPERSCALER: BofA takes **2020-2024 capex averaging ~21% of revenue** as the proxy for Meta's steady-state, ex-AI capital intensity, and treats everything ABOVE that line as AI. On Street capex that gives **incremental AI capex of $28bn (2025) → ~$82bn (2026, +190% y/y) → ~$95bn (2027) → ~$94bn (2028) = ~$300bn of incremental AI-focused capex over 2025-2028**, which BofA calls *"a fair estimate for incremental AI spend embedded in current Street ests"* while adding that Street capex *"has more upside than downside."*
  - 🔴🔴 **② THE BILL: at a **7-year average depreciable life** (weighted across chips, equipment, land and buildings), that $300bn implies **~$43bn of ANNUAL DEPRECIATION IN 2029** attributable to the 2025-28 AI build. BofA sanity-checks it a second way: *"if Meta wanted to finance the capex with 7% debt, and make a 10% return on the capex spend, Meta would need generate **$51bn in incremental profit**, which is in the ballpark of our $43bn depreciation estimate."*** ➤ **Two independent routes landing at $43-51bn is the most defensible "what does the AI build have to earn" number this page carries.**
  - 🔴🔴 **③ THE ANSWER, SOLVED THREE WAYS — THE REVENUE REQUIRED IN 2029 TO OFFSET THAT ~$43bn:**
    - **ADVERTISING — ~$61bn of incremental ad revenue**, at ~70% incremental margins ex-depreciation. For scale that is **~18% of consensus 2028 revenue of ~$343bn**.
    - **AI SUBSCRIPTIONS — ~$66bn**, at 65% margins including some app-store fees. At **$15/month that is 366mn incremental subscribers ≈ 10.5% of the current global daily active user base.**
    - **INFRASTRUCTURE / CLOUD — ~$86bn**, at 50% margins. For scale, Street has combined 2029 cloud revenue for [[AMZN]], [[GOOG]], [[MSFT]] and [[ORCL]] at **~$1 trillion**, so **Meta would need ~8-9% share of that market.**
  - 🔴 **④ AND BofA'S OWN VERDICT ON WHICH LEG CARRIES IT — *"ad revenues can do the bulk of the heavy lifting"*: Meta's 2020-24 ad revenue CAGR was ~18%; extrapolating that ex-AI to 2028 gives **~$311bn vs Street's ~$343bn = ~$32bn attributable to AI-driven improvement**. On a more conservative **15% ex-AI CAGR the 2028 base is ~$281bn, implying ~$62bn of AI-enabled ad revenue — "enough to cover incremental depreciation in 2029."*** ➤ **So the framework's own conclusion is that the ads business alone can just about pay for the build — but ONLY under the lower counterfactual growth rate. That sensitivity is the whole debate, and it is a testable one.**
  - ⚠️ **THE MARGIN CONSTRAINT THE SAME ARITHMETIC IMPLIES, which is the bear half: Street D&A **$19bn (2025) → $29bn (2026, +57%) → $44bn (2027, +49%) → $59bn (2028, +35%)**, taking D&A from **~9% of revenue in 2024 to ~17% by 2028**, against Street capex rising **$37bn (2024) → $159bn (2027), a 62% 3-year CAGR versus a 22% revenue CAGR.** To hold operating margins in the *"high to 30s to low 40s range"*, Meta *"would need to offset this increase through cost cutting other areas, implying a reduction in 2028 non-depreciation expenses by **7% of revenue** over the period, or roughly **$30bn**."* ⚠️ **All of these are STREET/Visible-Alpha figures as of June 2026 and are below what this page now carries post-print (FY26 guide $130-145bn, no FY27 guide) — use them for the STRUCTURE, not the level.**
  - 🔴 **⑤ THE COMPETITIVE FRAMEWORK THAT HAS NOT BEEN SUPERSEDED — BofA'S OPENAI AD-REALLOCATION MODEL, WHICH IS THE ONLY SIZED VERSION OF THIS RISK ON THE PAGE.** Per press reports citing investor materials, OpenAI's internal ad-revenue forecasts run **$11bn (2027) → $25bn (2028) → $53bn (2029) → $100bn (2030)**, underpinned by a modelled **~2.75bn weekly active users** by end-decade. BofA then splits it: the **incremental** share of those budgets rises **20% (2026) → 50% (2030)**, so **REALLOCATED** budgets go **$2bn (2026) → $7.7bn (2027) → $15bn (2028) → $29.2bn (2029) → $50bn (2030)** — equal to **0.3% of combined Google-properties + Meta + Amazon ad revenue in 2026, rising to ~4.8% by 2030 (5.6% of Google + Meta alone).** ⚠️ **This is a BofA ANALYSIS built on PRESS-REPORTED OpenAI internals, not an OpenAI disclosure. Carry the sourcing chain.**
  - 🔴 **⑥ AND THE TIKTOK GAP, WHICH IS THE SAME ARGUMENT FROM THE OTHER SIDE: per Sensor Tower (April 2026) TikTok has **~79mn US DAUs at ~80 minutes/day**, versus **~58 mins on Instagram, ~36 on Facebook, ~84 on YouTube** — *"TikTok's total US time spent is ~85% of total time spent on Instagram and Facebook combined."* Yet BofA's industry calls put **TikTok's 2025 US ad revenue at $10-12bn = ~11% of Meta's US ad revenue despite ~50% of the time spent.** BofA estimates rapid TikTok growth over the following year *"could lower ad growth for competitors by 1-2 points"*, with new inventory also pressuring industry pricing. ➤ **A monetisation gap that wide is either a durable Meta moat or the largest single pool of contestable ad dollars in the market. The 2026 deal completion removed the regulatory reason it stayed unmonetised.**
  - **⑦ TWO SMALLER DURABLE ITEMS: agentic workflows *"can consume 5x to 20x more tokens versus traditional single-pass inference"* (BofA's own exhibit puts multi-agent at >20x chat), which is the demand-side mechanism under the compute build; and **SilverTorch**, the late-May index-as-model recommendation architecture, is credited with **20.9x compute-cost efficiency versus CPU-based solutions** — the only quantified efficiency offset on this page against the D&A ramp.**
  - ⚠️ **WHAT IN THIS NOTE IS NOW WRONG OR OVERTAKEN, LISTED SO IT IS NOT RE-IMPORTED AS IF FRESH: (a) the **PO $835 / price $570.98 / "16x Street 2027 EPS"** valuation frame — superseded (see Changelog); (b) BofA's 2026 capex of **$130.3bn** in this model, lifted by the same analyst to **$145bn'26 / $185bn'27** four weeks later (07-07) and overtaken again by the $130-145bn company guide plus the ~$200bn-to-mid-$200bns 2027 range on this page; (c) the **Multistate youth-addiction trial *"scheduled to begin in Oakland, California, on August 6, 2026"*** — **SETTLED 2026-08-26**, see `## Current state`; (d) **Meta AI app DAUs doubling from 5mn (Mar'26) to ~10mn (May'26)** per Sensor Tower — directionally overtaken by BofA's own 08-31 framing that the app is *"capturing limited user activity relative to ChatGPT and Gemini"*; (e) the **Meta One Plus $7.99 / Meta One Premium $19.99** subscription tiers and the *"each 1% AI subscription conversion (~35mn users) at ~$10/month ≈ $4.2bn revenue"* sensitivity, which the 09-01 Muse Code paid-tier row now extends into developers.**
- **Bear (from BofA equity call 2026-06-11, multiple buy-side PMs):** (1) Capital intensity + lack of visibility — capex from ~$30B to ~$150B for ~10pts of incremental growth; **incremental ROIC deeply negative**, and the MSL lab is a hidden ~$40B/yr loss inside the P&L by '27 (per "super-bull" framing, Grant). (2) "Trust-the-founder" fatigue with three more megacap AI IPOs coming for the same capital. (3) No enterprise/cloud story to drive a re-rate the way GOOGL's TPU narrative did — "earnings beats won't re-rate it; needs a real AI story" _(NOTE: partially contested by the 2026-07-01 "Meta Compute" launch — see Debate bullet above; the enterprise/cloud vehicle bears wanted may now exist, though whether it re-rates the stock is still open)_. (4) Equity-raise overhang ("tens of billions," FT, Jun 5) seen as the near-term clearing event everyone wants resolved (Keith, same call). (5) Morale/talent: CTO Bosworth said internal morale "almost the worst it's ever been"; Head of Product for AI-for-Work resigned 2 months in; token-tracking + keystroke surveillance (Rothschild/Rowan Adley — Buy, briefing 2026-06-18; Nowak 2026-05-08). (6) Model gap: Llama 4 ranked #39 on LM Arena text leaderboard — open-source leadership lost (JPM OpenAI initiation, 2025-07).
- **Bear (Infra org needs a "culture reset" — execution/capital-discipline risk; SemiAnalysis "Meta's Infrastructure Team Needs A Culture Reset", 2026-07-21):** a detailed teardown arguing Meta Infrastructure has become **bloated and political** — middle managers over-engineer solutions to justify headcount/budget, the **six-month perf-review cycle (bottom 10-15% cut each round)** breeds short-term "window washing" projects that get abandoned, and supply-chain teams have little say over engineering, producing politically-driven rather than co-designed hardware. The pattern of expensive missteps: (1) the **>$2.5bn Rivos acquisition** (customer-owned tooling to bypass Broadcom) — a misstep since a COT team could be built for ~$100m/yr; ~30% of Rivos engineers already laid off, the Rivos-based "Olympus" chip cancelled, follow-on "Phoebe" not taping out until 2028; (2) **Grand Teton** (H100 server with an extra Broadcom-PCIe-switch tray) — cancelled after the extra storage went underutilized; (3) **Ariel** (the custom 1-GPU-per-Grace GB200 NVL36x2 rack Meta was the sole buyer of) — SemiAnalysis calculates **~14% higher TCO than a standard GB200 NVL72** and worse $/FLOP for LLMs, "cost Meta billions"; (4) a planned **cut-down (half-compute, 8-Hi HBM) custom MI450X** SemiAnalysis warns will "nuke AMD's volume at Meta"; (5) the over-engineered **DSF networking fabric** (~11% more expensive than the simpler NSF, single-vendor Arista lock-in) it later pivoted away from. Frequent pivots erode supplier trust — some suppliers now favor Amazon/Google designs over Meta's reshuffling. Explicit read-across to the **Reality Labs overspend** pattern (billions on R&D/headcount until layoffs). The bull rebuttal already on the page (MSL now "world-class at data+talent+compute," SemiAnalysis 07-09) is on the *research* org; this piece is a pointed negative on the *infrastructure* org's capital efficiency — directly feeding the capex/ROIC and morale/talent bear axes, and a warning as Meta pivots to selling compute externally ("customers likely won't pay for these over-engineered designs"). (SemiAnalysis "Meta's Infrastructure Team Needs A Culture Reset", 2026-07-21)
- **NEW and the sharpest single number on Meta's AI capex — the AMD warrant is an ~85% effective rebate on 6GW of Instinct (SemiAnalysis "Can AMD break the CUDA Moat? AMD Advancing AI 2026", 2026-07-24):** nothing on this page currently carries the *economics* of the AMD deal, only its silicon spec — and the economics are large enough to matter to the capex/ROIC debate that dominates the quarter. AMD issued Meta a performance warrant in **February 2026** (OpenAI's came October 2025) for **up to 160M AMD shares — ~10% of shares outstanding — at a $0.01 exercise price**, vesting in tranches against the **purchase and deployment of 6GW of Instinct**, with vesting price hurdles **stepping up to $600**. If AMD reaches $600 (the level satisfying all tranches), Meta records an **effective rebate of ~$16.0B/GW against a purchase price of ~$18.7B/GW — an ~85% partner discount**; OpenAI's parallel deal works out at **~105%** because it pays less per GW (~$15.2B/GW), such that "OpenAI could earn money simply by buying MI455X servers and exercising the accompanying warrants." Two caveats SemiAnalysis is explicit about: the warrants are **an incentive, not a commitment** (orders remain contingent on AMD delivering MI455X silicon and the Helios system), and the rebate only crystallises if AMD's stock actually reaches $600 while the holder still owns the shares. **Buy-side read:** none of the capex models on this page (BofA $145bn'26/$185bn'27; MS $175/$205bn; JPM ~$185/$225bn; buyside FY27 poll ~$204bn) appear to net any equity consideration against Meta's AI spend — if even a fraction of the warrant vests, the *economic* cost per AMD gigawatt is far below the accounting capex, which cuts directly against the "highly negative incremental ROIC" bear. It is also the mirror of the risk: Meta is taking equity in a supplier whose ramp is the thing that has to work.
  - **The same note hardens the S9 "Culture Reset" conclusion on the custom part, with the precise spec.** The **Meta agreement wraps a co-engineered custom Instinct variant around the MI455 platform** — the cut-down recsys part: **4 compute dies instead of 8, 6 HBM stacks instead of 12, and 8-Hi HBM4 in place of the standard 12-Hi** (which reconciles exactly with the ~144GB-vs-432GB figure already on the page from @SemiAnalysis_, 07-21 — a refinement, not a supersede). Halving compute and memory silicon raises the CPU:GPU ratio, "mirroring Nvidia's Meta-specific 'Ariel' variant of the GB200 NVL72" — the second time Meta has bought a depopulated flagship. The governance point is the one that matters: **the decision was made by the RecSys infrastructure team before TBD Lab was formed or could have its say**, **TBD has no interest in the system and it is unattractive to external customers**, and it "**is going to nuke AMD's volume at Meta because TBD will vastly prefer Rubin if the half MI455 design is chosen**." Note the direct tension with the Meta Compute thesis: a part that is unattractive to external customers is a part Meta cannot resell. **Encouraging coda, and the first sign the critique is landing internally:** "after our article, **Mark Zuckerberg has already began rapidly exploring changes to their infrastructure strategy org culture**" — i.e. the S9 governance bear now has a management-response datapoint attached to it. (SemiAnalysis, 2026-07-24) [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_07_24_ENC_Can_AMD_break_the_CUDA_Moat_AMD_Advancing_AI_2026_email.html)
- **MS puts numbers on the Muse-API thesis — a model-enabled API on OWNED infrastructure earns ~46% incremental ROIC, the best of the three business models it frameworks (Morgan Stanley "The Paths to 25-50% GenAI ROIC", Brian Nowak CFA / Stephen C Byrd / Adam Wood, 2026-07-27; Industry View Attractive):** the note that finally quantifies the leg of the META thesis this page has been carrying qualitatively since Nowak's 07-14 podcast ("a Muse API business ~$2+ of EPS, priced 30-80% below the private labs"). MS is "bullish… hyperscalers AMZN, GOOGL, MSFT and **model developers GOOGL and META**," and standardises three frameworks on **1GW of NVDA GB300 — 410,256 chips, $39bn all-in capex/GW ($23bn IT on a 5-yr life + $16bn non-IT on a 15-yr life, ~$4mn per GB300 rack per MS's Asia semis team's rack BoM) → ~$6bn/yr of depreciation, 75% utilisation, $7.6bn/GW of total opex, 21% tax.** **The META-relevant one is #2, the model-enabled API on owned infrastructure:** 65% of capacity to inference, **2,750 tokens/sec/GPU**, **$1.75/mn blended tokens → revenue $30bn/GW, incremental EBIT $22.7bn (~75% incremental margin), NOPAT $17.9bn, ~46% ROIC**, with the scenario band spanning **19% → 63% ROIC**. MS names "**META's recently announced API**" (alongside GOOGL Gemini and SPCX Grok) as exactly this business, and lists "**Meta Muse**" among the public model labs. **The single most META-specific number in the note, and it is a validation of Meta's pricing posture rather than of its model quality: MS's $1.75/mn blended token price is benchmarked to "Muse Spark 1.1 blended pricing $1.58 (8K/1K I/O)."** In other words the Street's reference price for the whole GenAI-API economy is now **Meta's own model**, which is the commercial expression of the "priced 30-80% below the private labs" strategy already on this page and of SemiAnalysis's 07-09 read that Muse Spark 1.1 is priced just under GLM 5.2. **For the Meta Compute leg, framework #3 is the relevant one — the same API run on 3P/RENTED infrastructure at $7.75/hr yields $40.5bn/GW of revenue less ~$28bn of compute cost = $12.6bn EBIT (~31% margin), $10.0bn NOPAT, ~25% NOPAT margin (band -9% → 32%).** Read the two together and MS has priced both sides of Meta's position: **~46% ROIC if Meta serves its own tokens on its own gigawatts, ~25% NOPAT margin for whoever rents, and ~31% ROIC / ~67% incremental EBIT margin for the landlord (framework #1, hyperscaler GPU rental at $8.50/hr → $22.9bn revenue/GW)** — i.e. the ~$40/Watt Meta Compute sensitivity already on this page now sits inside a published unit-economics stack. Methodology caveats worth carrying: the **2,000-3,500 tokens/sec/GPU** range is **not a hardware constant** (a DeepSeek-V4-Pro 1.6T, FP4, 8K/1K workload at ~50 tokens/sec/user; GB300 well ahead of GB200 at matched interactivity but varying by software stack); **larger models (2.8T Kimi-K3) price higher but throughput lower, so higher token pricing does NOT scale revenue/GW proportionately** — a direct caution against assuming a frontier-parity Muse would earn proportionately more; and training cost is handled by **discounting the compute available for revenue-generating inference while still bearing all the costs**, which is precisely how the MSL spend should be charged against the API ROIC. MS's closing corollary cuts both ways for Meta: these returns are "an invitation for competition… further new entrants/open source offerings," keeping the premium on product and compute-efficiency innovation and on **the value of compute infrastructure assets**. (Morgan Stanley "The Paths to 25-50% GenAI ROIC", 2026-07-27) [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/INTERNET_20260727_2024.html)
- **The refreshed MS risk-reward page for META — Top Pick reaffirmed, but MS's own model has GAAP operating income growing only ~3% in 2028 (Morgan Stanley "The Paths to 25-50% GenAI ROIC", 2026-07-27):** **Overweight, Top Pick, PT $775.00**, thesis titled **"The Age of Efficiency"** — no drift, this is the same $775 Nowak reiterated 07-01 and 07-06. Methodology: **DCF / long-term EBITDA multiple, ~8% WACC, ~3% terminal growth, implying ~23x '27 P/E**; **Bull $1,000** (implied ~29x blended '27/'28 P/E — AI investments drive engagement and new products, outsized ad monetisation, click-to-message execution, Reels-gap closure); **Bear $450** (implied ~15x — engagement declines and/or slower Reels monetisation, macro, ad-targeting regulation, and explicitly "**Higher capital intensity from mis-execution around the data center build widens losses with minimal ROIC**"). **Spot $595.19**; mean consensus PT **$823.38** (range **$664.46-$1,015**); consensus rating distribution **88% OW / 12% EW / 0% UW**; options-implied (24 Jul 2026) **P(>$1,000) ~3.6%, P(>$775) ~17.9%, P(<$450) ~27.6%** — the options market puts the bear case at ~1.5x the base, and MS's own $775 at under 1-in-5. The three "underappreciated call options" are restated as **1) further AI-driven upside, 2) subscription adoption, 3) click-to-message** — the same stack this page carries from the 06-03 "4 Products" note and the 07-14 podcast, now with the Muse API leg quantified separately (see the framework bullet above). ⚠️ **The number to argue with, and it is inside the bull's own model: MS's key-earnings-inputs table has GAAP operating income at 83,276 (2025) → 92,216 (26e) → 101,748 (27e) → 104,841 (28e) — that is +10.7% in 2026, +10.3% in 2027, and only +3.0% in 2028**, against constant-currency revenue growth of **22.5% (2025) → 26.0 (26e) → 22.0 (27e) → 19.8% (28e)**. Revenue compounding near 20% with operating income flat-lining at +3% is the depreciation wall from MS's own **$225bn/$250bn '27/'28 capex path** (already on this page from the 07-12 note) showing up in the P&L. **That is the "capex/ROIC" bear on this page — the BofA-call "incremental ROIC deeply negative" charge — expressed in the top-pick analyst's published estimates, and it is why the thesis rests on call options that are explicitly NOT in the base numbers.** Do not smooth it: MS's $775 and its own 2028 OI of $104,841m are held together only by the optionality. Other drivers: consolidated DAU **2,239.9 → 2,294.5 → 2,344.5 → 2,389.2mn**. MS vs consensus FY Dec-2026e: revenue **254,653 vs cons 253,077** (range 237,140-269,079); EBITDA **148,034 vs cons 142,872**; net income **84,622 vs cons 84,383**; **EPS 32.49 vs cons 32.95** (range 28.74-38.62) — i.e. MS is *above* consensus on revenue and EBITDA but *below* it on EPS, consistent with the heavier depreciation/interest load in its model. Ownership: Inst. owners 55.5% active, HF sector long/short 2.6x, net exposure 12.3%; MS Alpha Models 3/5 BEST (24m) / 2/5 MOST (3m). (Morgan Stanley "The Paths to 25-50% GenAI ROIC", 2026-07-27) [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/INTERNET_20260727_2024.html)
- 🔴 **NEW BEAR EVIDENCE ON THE OPEN-WEIGHTS LEG, AND IT IS MEASURED RATHER THAN ARGUED — LLAMA'S DISTRIBUTION POSITION IS ERODING TO QWEN DESPITE IDENTICAL SHELF SPACE (Hugging Face · Adina Yakefu / Apolinário / Irene Solaiman, "State of Open Models: Summer 2026 Observations", 2026-08-14).** This page's open-source thesis — that Meta's open-weight strategy buys ecosystem position and developer mindshare in lieu of frontier leadership — now has a scoreboard, and Meta is losing it on the metric that matters. **Qwen-based models account for 151,448 DERIVATIVES on the Hub — 2.6x META'S TOTAL FOOTPRINT and 4.7x THE LLAMA REPOSITORIES SPECIFICALLY**, with Qwen derivatives growing **~180–210 new repos per day**; [[GOOG]] is second at 82,506. **On actual usage: GGUF downloads per month run QWEN 39.6M vs GEMMA 20.8M vs LLAMA 7.5M.**
- 🔴 **THE SENTENCE THAT KILLS THE EASIEST REBUTTAL, quoted: "The Llama gap is NOT A SUPPLY PROBLEM, Llama-derived GGUF repositories SLIGHTLY OUTNUMBER QWEN'S. SAME SHELF SPACE, A FIFTH OF THE TRAFFIC."** ➜ So this is not an availability, packaging or tooling gap — developers can get Llama builds just as easily and choose Qwen anyway. That is a **preference/quality verdict on the models themselves**, which is the one explanation the "open-weights-as-distribution" bull case cannot absorb. ⚠️ **Second qualifier in the same direction: only 9% OF META'S 2026 HUB DOWNLOADS are of models above 70B** (NVDA 14%; [[GOOG]], MSFT and IBM Granite essentially none; against MiniMax ~all, Moonshot 88%, DeepSeek 55%) — Meta's open franchise is a small-model franchise. **HF does note Meta "reignites open roots" with MUSE GLIMMER** (already on this page as the 30B single-consumer-GPU release), so the direction of travel is acknowledged — but the traffic has not followed yet.
- ⚠️ **HOW TO HOLD IT: Hub derivatives and GGUF pulls measure DEVELOPER/LOCAL-INFERENCE distribution, not enterprise API revenue, not Meta AI consumer usage, and not ad-ranking model quality — none of which appear in this dataset. It is nevertheless the cleanest published measure of the ecosystem-position claim the bull case rests on, and it currently reads AGAINST Meta. Pair it with the MS 08-12 read already on the page (open weights compress model-layer pricing, and MS's preference sentence excludes META) — two independent sources now say the open-weights leg is a cost, not a moat.**
- **🆕🔴 SUPPLY-CHAIN EVIDENCE AGAINST THE META-SLOWDOWN HEADLINES — an independent check on a narrative rather than a new number (BofA Asia semis + components conference call, 2026-08-20; BofA Asia tech team — host "Robert", semis "Haas", components "Doris"; tech-tour and earnings-season feedback).** The companies attending BofA's Asia tech tour *"are **not seeing any of the slowdown** from hyperscaler investment **despite the fact that there has been some news talking about the Google as well as Meta might slowing down their investment or cutting their head count in the frontier model development**."* Hyperscaler capex is still trending up, although the growth rate *"could actually be trending more modestly."* ⚠️ **HOW TO USE IT: this is CHANNEL FEEDBACK from Asian suppliers, not company guidance and not a Meta datapoint in the ordinary sense. Its value is that the "Meta is slowing down / cutting frontier-model headcount" story — which is doing real work in the bear case on this page alongside the May RIF, the MSL-ROIC complaint and the "no 2027 capex guide" vacuum — is NOT visible in the order books of the companies that would see it first.** ➤ **That does not vindicate the spend; it narrows the debate to ROI rather than volume. The supply chain says the money is still being spent.** ⚠️ **Note the honest limit: suppliers see bookings, not intent, and a decision taken in August would not show up in an Asian component order book for another two quarters.**
  - **The mechanism BofA attaches to it matters as much as the datapoint, because it is a MIX call that bears directly on Meta's silicon strategy: the shift toward ASICs over GPUs into 2H26/2027, because *"hyperscalers are going to spend every single piece of their money in a more disciplined way… the component cost especially in **memory** is just getting higher, while they will **not be able to fully fulfil their own capex with their free cash flow as well as cash position** as compared with one to two years ago"* — forcing external capital raises and pushing them toward **monetising models rather than more frontier-training GPU spend.** ➤ **Read that against the two live threads on this page: (1) Susan Li's deferral of the financing question to the 2027 planning cycle ("we don't have any plans to share"; OCF "the primary pillar") — BofA's Asia channel is saying external capital is now structurally required across the cohort, not optionally available; and (2) the "AI monetization roadmap" row, where the stock broke on the absence of specifics. BofA's framing says the whole cohort is being pushed the same way Meta is being asked to go.** (BofA Asia semis + components call, 2026-08-20) [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_08_20_baml_call.html)
- **🆕 META NAMED AS ONE OF THE TWO FLAGSHIP AMD "HELIOS" CUSTOMERS — logged as a second-source-identified customer relationship, NOT an announced deal (J.P. Morgan Asia hardware call, 2026-08-20).** On AMD's Helios rack platform: *"the two major projects — one is Oracle, another is **Meta**"*, both in progress, with **mass production around Q1 2027** and rack-level shipment *"more like first half next year."* ⚠️⚠️ **SOURCE QUALITY, STATED BECAUSE IT IS BAD: this transcript carries a severe ASR defect — **13,071 `[silence]` filler lines around roughly 20KB of real content** — and names in it are unreliable. Treat the Meta attribution as a supply-chain identification requiring confirmation, not as a disclosed customer win, and do not build a unit or revenue number off it.** ➤ **Why it is still worth logging: Meta has no announced Helios commitment on this page, and a second-source identification of Meta as one of two flagship customers alongside Oracle is a data point about the ASIC-and-alternative-silicon diversification that BofA's same-day call says the whole cohort is being pushed toward. It also dates the ramp — Q1 2027 mass production — which is inside the window where Meta has declined to guide capex.** (J.P. Morgan Asia hardware call, 2026-08-20) [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_08_20_nw_jpm_20_ago_26.html)

MS OW $775 reiterated post-Meta-Compute (Nowak, 2026-07-01), **Citi initiates/reiterates Buy $850** — highest PT on the page — on the Meta Compute news (Josey, 2026-07-01), BofA Buy $835 (Post) — **and BofA ADDED META to its US 1 best-ideas list (US 1 Committee, 2026-07-09)**, Barclays OW $770 (US Internet & Semis), Redburn/R&Co Buy "Top Idea" (Adley, briefing 2026-05-26).
