**Signal vs management** — what management said on the last call × what the intra-quarter flow is saying (✓ confirms · ⚠ nuances · ✗ contests):

_One-line synthesis (updated 2026-07-16): the "Meta Compute" launch is the biggest signal shift of the quarter — it directly answers the "no enterprise/cloud vehicle" bear/contests point below, converts Nowak's "backup optionality" into an active business, but opens a new capex-uncertainty axis (buyside 2027E spans $175-275B) and a new competitive-risk axis (neocloud names). **07-06 MS (Joe Moore) reframes the overcapacity fear as "excess demand, not excess supply"** (GPU still short; FUNDA: it's older-gen Hopper being resold, not next-gen idled), and Nowak keeps the neocloud lever as ~$3+/8% EPS upside while insisting the core thesis is product, not neocloud; separately the 'Watermelon' GPT-5.5-parity claim (MS TMT 07-06) is the first datapoint pushing back on the "model clearly behind" bear. **NEW (07-08/09): the overcapacity bear inverts outright — Reuters reports META plans to ~2x compute to ~14GW next year with Bernstein hearing ~$220B total 2027 capex (the newest escalation, corroborating BofA $185bn / MS $175bn), META is breaking ground on a $10B/1GW Canada DC, MS TMT relays "high capex intact," and BofA added META to its US 1 list — while JEF/@alexeheath say Meta AI expects to surpass Opus-level this year with the frontier model in training, further narrowing the model gap.** Net: **re-rate case strengthens, overcapacity bear now largely inverted (the debate has flipped from "too much compute" to "doubling compute"), ROIC-visibility/financing debate does not resolve — it just changes shape (equity-raise-imminence question resurfaces).** SemiAnalysis (07-09) adds the structural MSL bull: Meta is the only shop on track to be world-class at data+talent+compute (best shot at #3, "the race for 3rd is Meta vs SpaceX, not Google"), with a ~3,000-engineer RL-data org, Muse Spark 1.1 now ~Opus 4.6/GLM 5.2, and compute projected to exceed OpenAI+Anthropic by end-2026 — but "the slope, not the intercept," parity not yet here (no token volume moved to Muse, no Anthropic/OpenAI parity expected until end-2026). **NEW (07-10/11): the sentiment reset completes** — @dnystedt/Reuters put a September production date on in-house 'Iris' silicon (4-gen MTIA; Broadcom design / TSMC fab; Samsung/SanDisk/Sumitomo supply deals) and a ~7GW(2026)→~14GW(2027) compute plan; Vital Knowledge (Crisafulli, 07-11) calls the last-72h feedback "as bullish as Google after Gemini 3" and ranks Meta #3 in the frontier pecking order (Anthropic/OpenAI/Meta/Google/SpaceX); and two bull framings (AlphaSense GOOGL-employee expert: "doesn't need to win the model race"; @RihardJarc: market "priced for zero") argue the BofA "little-to-negative value per AI GW" gap is the upside. Net: monetization-vehicle bear largely resolved, model-parity + financing/equity-raise the residual debates. **07-13/14 adds:** CNBC (via @firstadopter) confirms the Richland Parish escalation to 5GW/>$50B (vs the $27B revealed in October) — "increasing, not lowering" DC spend — with @FundaAI calling "Meta Next-Year Capex Defies Cut Fears" the week's biggest narrative change; @Hedgeye flags ~$700B combined AI-DC debt across the big-5 (feeds the financing/equity-raise axis); and @alexandr_wang's Muse Spark 1.1 benchmark run (#3 Debate Benchmark, SOTA RadLE-H/HealthBench, perfect APhO) further chips at the model-parity gap. **POST-PRINT (07-29 call, ingested 07-30): the quarter closes with the ads-AI leg CONFIRMED and quantified (+8.3% clicks / +15.7% FB conversions, Advantage+ >$75B run-rate, Meta AI daily interactors +60%), the monetisation vehicles all shipping but with ZERO disclosed revenue, and the capital question deliberately left open — no FY27 capex guide, no equity raise, FY26 narrowed not raised, funding answered with "a greater mix of debt" plus the BlackRock 1GW El Paso JV. The FY27 capex spread (~$145bn / ~$205bn / $250bn+) therefore survives the print intact. The one axis the call does settle is the overcapacity premise: Li says Meta is "demand-constrained" with "numerous ROI-positive places that we would put Compute toward if we had it," and Zuck that "there's just nowhere near enough Compute for all the demand" — even while confirming "offers for Compute at a significant premium over what we paid."**_

**Quarter synthesis — Q2'26 print (call 2026-07-29, ingested 2026-07-30):** the print did what the flow expected on the P&L and refused what the flow wanted on capital. **Revenue $60.8B +28%** with the growth mix rotating from volume to price, and — the genuinely new content — **LLMs are now measurably inside the ads stack**: the "Meta generative recommender" paradigm shift, the first generative model live in ads retrieval, **+8.3% ad clicks and +15.7% conversion uplift on Facebook** from GEM plus sequence learning, and **Advantage+ end-to-end at >$75B annual run-rate** (from >$60B at Q3'25) across **9M SMBs**. That is the direct answer to the page's year-long "the growth is pre-AI-product" bear (BofA equity call, 06-11). On the product side the monetisation vehicles all exist now — **public Muse API + OpenRouter, Meta One subscriptions, Business Agents at >1M businesses/week, Meta AI daily interactors +60%** — but with **zero disclosed revenue attached to any of them**, and **personal agents remain unshipped**, keeping Nowak's Connect-by-September test (Sept 23) live. On capital the print is a non-event by design: **no FY27 capex guide** ("highly dynamic"), FY26 **narrowed** rather than raised (so BofA's expected $135-150bn raise did not materialise), **no equity raise**, and the funding answer given as "a greater mix of debt" plus the **BlackRock 1GW El Paso JV** — which leaves the page's three-way FY27 spread (Redburn ~$145bn / Street ~$200-205bn / Bernstein's whispered $250bn+) **completely unresolved into a widening-CDS backdrop**. The single most valuable line for the July debate is Li's: **Meta is "demand-constrained," with "numerous ROI-positive places that we would put Compute toward if we had it"** — first-party confirmation of the MS/Joe Moore "excess demand, not excess supply" read and a direct rebuttal of the premise behind the early-July neocloud/semis/memory sell-off, even as Zuckerberg confirms **"offers for Compute at a significant premium over what we paid for it."** His hierarchy — **"significantly higher margin on selling intelligence rather than selling Compute directly"** — is the right frame for every $/GW model on this page: compute-selling is the floor, not the thesis, and the enterprise GTM to deliver the rest is admittedly "somewhat of a new muscle."

| 07-09 | **🔴 Wells Fargo · Ken Gawrelski — "Meta Platforms: AI Developments—Key Takeaways" (flash, PRIMARY PDF, ingested 2026-08-29)** | capital | **bull** | **★ THE ORIGINAL SOURCE OF THE "7GW × $40bn/GW" ARITHMETIC THAT THIS PAGE LATER CARRIES SECOND-HAND VIA BofA/Post (08-27) — here it is first-hand, and the capex sensitivity grid is the useful part. OVERWEIGHT, PT $767.00 (up from $765 on 06-29), price $631.48 (07/09/2026), +21.5% upside, 52-wk $520.26-796.25, mkt cap $1,619,115m.** **On the Reuters story (META to double compute to 14GW by YE27): WF reads it as *"generally IN LINE w/ our capacity forecasts informed by public power purchase agreements"* — WF estimates Meta ended '25 with 7.5GW owned compute expanding to 16GW by YE27, i.e. 3-4GW net new per year for '26 and '27, or ~7GW of net adds over 18-24 MONTHS. ⚠️ The bull/bear hinges entirely on whether the 7GW is an 18-24-month figure or a 2027-ALONE figure — WF calls the latter *"aggressive, but not impossible."*** 🔴 **THE CAPEX SENSITIVITY (Exhibit 4), which is what makes the note worth keeping: at ~$40bn/GW (WF's estimate for Meta, factoring capacity mix and supply-chain inflation), 4.0GW of '27 adds = $160bn and 7.0GW = $280bn; the published grid spans $90bn (3.0GW × $30bn) to $350bn (7.0GW × $50bn). WF's own '27 estimate is $180bn, and it reads market expectation as *"roughly $200B… unlikely to move appreciably on disclosures."*** ⚠️ **Note the internal cross-check WF supplies for the denominator: *"2026 CapEx guidance of $125-$140B suggests $35B-$40B/GW."*** ⚠️⚠️ **UNIT DISCIPLINE: this $40bn/GW is a TOTAL BUILD COST per GW. It is not revenue-per-GW and must not be netted against Redburn's $13-25bn revenue-per-GW (07-30 row) — same denominator, different quantity. The standing `$/watt` rule on this wiki applies.** 🔴 **THE MONETISATION LEG, AND IT IS A HARD NUMBER THIS PAGE OTHERWISE ONLY HAS QUALITATIVELY: Zuckerberg confirmed Meta is CONSIDERING renting compute externally *"given potentially superior return profile,"* while DENYING any "excess" compute — and WF sizes it: *"we believe the LEASE OF 1GW OF COMPUTE COULD YIELD 16% ACCRETION TO FY27 EPS,"* with Meta in position to lease 1GW+ in early '27 *"without foreclosing upon model & application ambitions."*** **Product: Muse Spark 1.1 with paid developer tiers and "standout" agentic improvements; Zuckerberg called it *"better than we expected"* with reports it outperforms leading Gemini models; frontier model "Watermelon" still expected.** 🔴 **PRICING READ-THROUGH, logged to [[ANTHROPIC]] / [[OPENAI]]: *"META will launch model API system priced at 25% THE COST of leading Anthropic and OpenAI offerings."*** **Read-through logged to [[GOOG]]: *"believe ONLY GOOGLE will deliver MORE THAN 7GW of capacity in '27, powered by TPU capacity."*** (Wells Fargo Securities · Ken Gawrelski w/ Morrissey, Umansky, Ashkar, Rusnak, Rao, Brondolo, 2026-07-09) [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/f03e29b2-1bb1-4bc5-ab07-c4a1dd002273.html) |

**Quarter synthesis:** the print was strong (+33% pre-AI-product), but the debate shifted from ad growth to the capex×ROIC equation — JPM models negative FCF and a ~$97B funding gap, while bears see MSL as a hidden ~$40B/year loss. The central controversy became the absence of an enterprise/cloud vehicle that would unlock a GOOG-style re-rate (Wigg/Thill), with the AI monetization upside (Business Agents) still seen as a 2027 story (Linzer). On Jun-24, the confirmation of META as the first big-tech customer for QCOM's C1000 CPU (datacenter, 2028) adds a layer of stack diversification that the market is watching as a signal of TCO discipline. On Jun-29, the FT reported Google **capped Meta's Gemini access in March** over its own capacity constraints — an unhelpful headwind to Meta's AI-efficiency push and to Business Agent monetization (compounding the Manus-deal unwind), and a fresh data point on how compute-constrained even the hyperscalers are. On Jun-30, Rothschild flagged Meta restricting internal use of Claude Code/Codex over distillation concerns (shifting to "MetaCode"), and JPM's desk described a pre-print bear overhang of "highly negative" perceived ROIC on the Superintelligence Lab plus an equity-issuance overhang. **Then on Jul-1 the debate was reset by Bloomberg's "Meta Compute" report** — Meta launching an external AI-cloud business to sell surplus capacity, directly answering the "no enterprise/cloud vehicle" bear point. Citi initiated the highest PT on the page (Buy $850) on the news; MS reiterated OW $775 but re-sized its neocloud optionality from "backup" (~$10B/GW, May) to an active $40B/GW-equivalent sensitivity worth $3+/8%+ of 2028 EPS. The stock moved +6-11% intraday while semis/memory sold off ~4-11% on a reignited capex-demand debate elsewhere in the chain. The news does NOT resolve the capex/ROIC uncertainty — if anything JPM's buyside color shows 2027E capex estimates are now more dispersed ($175-275B) than before — but it opens a genuinely new, previously-absent monetization vehicle, at the cost of a new competitive-risk axis versus CoreWeave/Nebius and other neoclouds. Finally, on Jul-9 SemiAnalysis published a full MSL progress update making the *structural* bull case — Meta as the only hyperscaler/neolab on track to be world-class at all three of data (the new ~3,000-engineer RL-data org + screen tracking), talent, and compute (projected to exceed OpenAI+Anthropic by end-2026), and therefore the best shot at the #3 frontier spot ("the race for 3rd is Meta vs SpaceX, not Google") — while cautioning that this is "the slope, not the intercept" (Muse Spark 1.1 only just reached Opus 4.6/GLM 5.2, no Anthropic/OpenAI parity expected until end-2026) and flagging the reverse tell: a no-clawback compute-sale deal, disbanding the RL org, or letting top researchers walk would be a "death sentence" for MSL. **07-10 — the re-rate case gets its clearest public articulation yet:** Zuckerberg laid out a *credible* plan to monetize the capex via two proven routes — **Neocloud + LLM API** — prompting JPM (Schilsky) to declare "META is Building a Hyperscaler" and note the debate has "MASSIVELY changed over the past two weeks" (stock +5%; MS TMT called it a "major AI vibe shift," META +4.7%). Reuters directly confirmed Meta will **start manufacturing its own AI chip from September** en route to ~14GW next year (Rothschild: 14GW by end-2027), and Meta debuted **Muse Spark 1.1 at the frontier priced aggressively low ($1.25/$4.25 input/output, below GLM 5.2)** — management framing it "very low cost" and calling rival pricing "very extreme," a strategy Blayne (JEF) flags as sustainable because META can run inference at cost while monetizing other businesses (unlike the pre-IPO labs chasing profitability). Net: the monetization-vehicle bear point that dominated the quarter is now largely resolved — the open questions narrow to model parity (still trailing to end-2026) and whether token growth outruns the frontier price deflation META itself is accelerating. **A supplier-side confirm lands 07-07:** on the Broadcom (Hock Tan) call, Broadcom names Meta a top-5 frontier builder + MTIA customer, says **there is no reason Meta couldn't resell MTIA to other frontier developers** (full PyTorch stack) — backing the Meta-Compute-as-merchant-cloud thesis from the chip vendor's mouth — and **dismisses the "oversupply of compute" concern for 27/28**, aligning with the "excess demand not excess supply" camp. Gokul (07-08) adds the packaging detail that **MTIA 400/450 moves to TSMC SOIC in 2H'26 or 2028**. **07-10/11 completes the sentiment reset:** @dnystedt (citing Reuters) puts a **September production date on in-house 'Iris' silicon** (4-generation MTIA; **Broadcom chief design partner, TSMC fab**) plus a **~7GW-2026 → ~14GW-2027 compute plan** and named long-term supply deals (**Samsung memory, SanDisk flash, Sumitomo fiber**) — hard supply-chain detail behind the compute-doubling story; Vital Knowledge (Crisafulli, 07-11) reports the last-72h buy-side feedback is **"as bullish as Google after Gemini 3,"** ranking Meta **#3 in the frontier pecking order** (Anthropic/OpenAI/Meta/Google/SpaceX); and two bull framings (an AlphaSense GOOGL-employee expert arguing Meta **"doesn't need to win the model race,"** and @RihardJarc arguing the market **"priced for zero"** on Meta's DC/AI build given its compute+talent+data edge) reframe the BofA "little-to-negative value per AI GW" discount as the upside case. @garrytan separately calls **Muse Spark 1.1 ("Hornbill") "really good."** **07-12 — where the buyside actually sits:** a BofA bogey poll (Fenske) pegs the buyside-avg FY27 capex bogey at **$204B** with the freshest post-'14GW-memo' conversations at **$215-230B** (vs Post's maintained $185B), alongside buyside EPS bogeys of Q2 GAAP **$7.53**, FY26 **$33.37** / FY27 **$36.20** and Q2/Q3 rev-growth bogeys of **+28%/+24%** — the capex debate remains the live axis into the print (BofA · Fenske / Post, 2026-07-12). **07-20 (into the print):** the credit market flinches even as the equity bulls hold — **JPM (Anmuth) DOWNGRADES META credit overweight→neutral** (modeling +42% capex to ~$200bn, a 1x net-leverage trigger, no back-to-back debt issuance), while **BofA (Post) keeps PO $835** and lays out a SOTP (~$600 core + ~$250 capacity) with external compute-sales placeholders of $5bn'27/$11bn'28, and **Octahedron Capital (07-15 LP call) established a new 5-10% META position** — calling the AI ROIC for a non-hyperscaler platform "highly positive" with 20%+ topline. Citrini's 07-20 note adds the aggressive-pricing datapoint (Muse blended ~$1/token, "90% cost savings vs Anthropic"). Net: the capital-structure/credit axis tightens while the buy-side and BofA increasingly underwrite the capacity build as monetizable optionality. **07-20/22 (previews into the 07-29 print):** BofA (Post/Bansal) reiterates Buy/$835 and models a 2Q beat ($60.6bn/$7.50 vs Street $60.2bn/$7.18) on the 10% May RIF + job-postings -49% q/q, flags a possible capex-guide raise to $135-150bn on memory, and adds $5bn to 2027 revenue post the Anthropic compute deal (→ $316bn rev / $35.00 EPS, ~19x Street '27 GAAP, below the ~21x 10-yr avg). On silicon, SemiAnalysis details Meta's custom **AMD MI400** as ~half a normal MI455X (~144GB HBM, recsys / $-per-bandwidth-optimized), while @dylan522p sharpens the 07-21 Infra "Culture Reset" bear into a "public cry to not waste the silicon and have better AI infra" — the Infra capital-efficiency/governance debate now runs alongside the (bullish) compute-monetization one. R&Co Redburn adds a product/monetization positive: Meta developing an 'OpenRouter' equivalent to cut inference costs. **07-24 — the AMD deal's ECONOMICS finally land on the page, and they cut against the ROIC bear.** SemiAnalysis's Advancing-AI teardown discloses that Meta's **February-2026 AMD warrant covers up to 160M shares (~10% of AMD outstanding) at a $0.01 strike**, vesting against the purchase and deployment of **6GW of Instinct** with hurdles to **$600** — at which point Meta books an **effective rebate of ~$16.0B/GW versus a ~$18.7B/GW purchase price, an ~85% partner discount** (OpenAI ~105%). Nothing in the capex stack the page tracks (BofA $145/$185bn, MS $175/$205bn, JPM ~$185/$225bn, buyside FY27 ~$204bn) nets equity consideration against the spend, so the *economic* cost per AMD gigawatt is materially below reported capex — the first hard offset to the "incremental ROIC deeply negative" bear, subject to the two caveats SemiAnalysis flags (warrants are an incentive not a commitment; the rebate needs AMD at $600). The same note hardens the governance bear rather than relieving it: the **custom MI455 variant (4 compute dies vs 8, 6 HBM stacks vs 12, 8-Hi vs 12-Hi HBM4** — reconciling the ~144GB-vs-432GB figure already on the page**)** was chosen by the **RecSys infra team before TBD Lab existed**, TBD has no interest in it, it is **unattractive to external customers** (hence not resellable under Meta Compute), and it "is going to nuke AMD's volume at Meta because TBD will vastly prefer Rubin." Net: the capex is cheaper than it looks and the silicon strategy is still the weak link — with one genuinely new positive, that **Zuckerberg "has already began rapidly exploring changes to their infrastructure strategy org culture"** after the 07-21 piece, the first management-response datapoint attached to the Infra-governance bear.
